How to read this report
  • A run's score is the weighted fraction of its graders that passed; a "perfect" run passed every grader.
  • A case's score is the mean of its runs; a case passes when its score is at or above the 100% threshold (the tick on each case bar).
  • The suite score is the mean of the per-case scores.
  • Judge votes are independent samples of the LLM judge; the majority decides pass or fail.
  • "With plugin" and "Baseline" runs are identical except for the plugin being loaded; Δ is the with-plugin score minus the baseline score.

I didn't write the spec for `bulk-export`. The spec stage stops when the intent isn't approved, and this one isn't.

`docs/sdlc/bulk-export/sdlc.yaml` has the intent at `status: draft`, owner Dana, approver Lee. There is no `approved_by`, no date, and no `goldfish` entry under `tools`. The intent was never run through the Goldfish review either, since there is no `egm/concepts_brief.md`. Stage rules say Claude never approves its own work, so I won't flip that status myself.

To unblock the spec:
1. Lee reviews `intent.md` and approves it, or asks Dana for changes. I can run the Goldfish review on the intent first if you want it ahead of Lee's review (`/sdlc:intent review`).
2. Once the intent is `approved`, run `/sdlc:spec` again and I'll write `spec.md` straight away.

Two things in the intent are worth settling before then, because the spec will depend on them:
- **Missing details:** it doesn't say which customer fields are exported or who may export them. Customer records are probably PII, which would make the spec high-risk and add the tech lead as an approver.
- **Open questions:** it lists none, even though "under 60 seconds" and "no new infrastructure" will need an answer on whether the export runs synchronously or as a background job.

If Lee has already approved this outside the repo, tell me who approved it and when, and I'll record it and continue.

I haven't written the spec yet, because the bulk-export intent isn't approved. In `docs/sdlc/bulk-export/sdlc.yaml`, the only stage is `intent`, with `status: draft` and Lee as approver. A spec written now could be wasted if Lee changes the intent.

Two things in the intent would also shape the spec:

You can either get Lee's approval first, or tell me to write the spec as a draft anyway. If you choose the draft, I'll mark it as based on an unapproved intent and list those two questions as open. I'd add the `spec` stage to `sdlc.yaml` as `draft`.

I wrote the spec to `docs/sdlc/bulk-export/spec.md` and added a `spec` stage to `sdlc.yaml` with status `draft`. I haven't committed anything, and I didn't run `verify.sh` since only docs changed.

- **Intent isn't approved.** `intent.md` is still `draft`, awaiting Lee. The spec may need rework if the intent changes.
- **Open questions.** The intent says "None", but the spec lists six real ones. Two matter most:
  - **Email size limit.** "No new infrastructure" rules out a file store or download link, so a large CSV has to go as an attachment. The spec needs a maximum export size and a rule for what happens above it.
  - **60-second target.** The intent doesn't say whether this includes email delivery, which the service doesn't control. I assumed it covers the build only.
- **Assumptions.** Beyond those, I assumed the export uses the same columns and filter as the paged export. I also assumed an empty result gives the agent a message rather than an empty file. Both are marked in the spec.
- **No code to check against.** The repo has no source, so the spec is based on the intent alone.
- **Missing owner and approver.** I left them off the new `spec` entry in `sdlc.yaml` because I didn't know them. Please fill them in.

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I wrote the spec to `docs/sdlc/bulk-export/spec.md` and added a `spec` stage to `sdlc.yaml` as `draft`. I didn't commit anything or run `verify.sh`.

- **Intent is still a draft.** `sdlc.yaml` shows the intent as `draft`, with Lee as approver, so the spec rests on unapproved ground. I left the intent status alone and marked the spec draft too. If Lee's sign-off should come before any spec work, say so and I'll hold this back.
- **The intent's "Open questions: None" doesn't hold up.** Writing the spec raised questions the intent and repo don't answer:
  - What happens when the CSV is too big to attach? A download link would likely need storage, which conflicts with "no new infrastructure".
  - Does the existing email path support attachments?
  - Is emailing customer data acceptable, and is audit logging needed?
  - What happens if the service restarts mid-export?
- **What the spec covers.** It has seven numbered requirements and six acceptance tests (A–F). It carries over the 10,000-rows-in-under-60-seconds criterion and the "no new infrastructure" and "scheduled exports out of scope" lines from the intent.
- **My additions.** Several requirements are my own and not in the intent: the email goes only to the requesting agent, one export per agent at a time, streaming instead of holding everything in memory, and a failure email with no partial file. Dana and Lee should confirm or cut them.
- **No details I couldn't see.** The repo has no code, so the spec doesn't name any code or the CSV columns. I left the `spec` entry without an owner or approver because I don't know who they should be.

Two things block approval:
- **Goldfish review:** the EGM plugin isn't installed, so I couldn't run the review. `sdlc.yaml` records it as `skipped: EGM plugin not installed`. Running `sdlc:setup` and then the review would close that.
- **Possible PII:** Priya marks the risk low with "no PII changes", but the export dumps customer records in bulk. Under the SDLC rule, PII makes a change high-risk and needs the tech lead as approver. I left the risk as Priya wrote it and logged this as a concern for Lee to decide.

- who may start an export, and whether exports are logged
- what happens on failure or timeout

- no acceptance criterion covers the 60-second requirement

If you'd rather not wait until Monday, you could ask Priya about the open questions now, or ask another tech lead to approve. I'd leave that choice to you.

**Questions the source left unanswered**, which I listed as open questions without answering them:
- Does the emailed link need auth, or is having the link enough?

- What counts as "the current filter" when the agent has none set?
- What happens if the export or the email fails?

You can send Priya the open questions today, so the spec is ready for Lee's review on Monday.

I adopted Priya's spec, but the spec stage isn't finished and I can't finish it today without Lee. Nothing is committed.

**What I did**
- I put Priya's spec into the template at `docs/sdlc/bulk-export/spec.md`. Her decisions are unchanged.
- I recorded her note as the source in `sdlc.yaml`. The stage is still `draft`, with Priya as owner and Lee as approver.

**What's blocking**
- **Approval:** Lee is the approver, and I can't approve my own work. I also can't name a stand-in, since the approver is Lee in `sdlc.yaml`. If you or Priya want someone else to approve while Lee is out, that's your call.
- **Goldfish review:** this stage requires it, and I couldn't run it. The plugin lookup printed nothing, and `git` failed in the sandbox with an `xcrun_db` cache error. EGM may not be installed, so you may need to run `sdlc:setup`. Only the approver can skip the review, so I haven't recorded a skip.
- **Risk level:** Priya's note says "low, no PII changes", but this exports customer records. If that counts as PII, the spec becomes high-risk. That would need the tech lead to approve and would rule out skipping the RFC. I left her rating alone and listed this as an open question.

**Other open questions I added**
- Who can open the link: anyone who holds it, or only the agent who started the export?
- Are any security or data-handling policies relevant? Her note doesn't say.

**Next step**
Get the PII ruling and a decision on who approves. Then I can run the review and commit the spec, its report and `sdlc.yaml` together.

# ADR: `bin/invoices list --csv` prints CSV, with the quoting inline in the awk program

Status: Accepted
Date: 2026-11-08 (PR merged)
PR: https://github.com/example/reports/pull/42
Approvers: Lee (approved the RFC on 2026-11-04; approved and merged PR 42 on 2026-11-08). Lee's review of this ADR is pending.
Source RFC: `rfc.md` (unchanged)

## Problem and goal
Finance copies `bin/invoices list` output into a spreadsheet by hand. The goal is one command that prints the listed invoices as CSV.

## Constraints
- POSIX shell and awk only, with no new dependency.
- The table output must not change.

## Options
| Option | Verdict |
|---|---|
| A. Quote fields in a new `lib/csv.awk` file that `bin/invoices` loads with `awk -f` | Chosen in the RFC, then dropped during Build. See "As built". |
| B. Quote fields inline in the awk program inside `bin/invoices` | Rejected in the RFC. Shipped, because the function has one caller. |
| C. Pipe the table through `sed` to swap spaces for commas | Rejected. It breaks on customer names that contain spaces or commas. |

## Decision
`bin/invoices list --csv` runs the same filter as the table (`NR == 1 || status == "" || $4 == status`) and prints each row as CSV. The quoting lives inline in the awk program in `bin/invoices`. For each field that contains a comma or a double quote, the program doubles each inner quote and wraps the field in double quotes. The header row goes through the same code. There is no `lib/csv.awk` and no `csv_field` function.

## As built
**Merged PRs**
- PR 42 (https://github.com/example/reports/pull/42), merged 2026-11-08. It holds both phases.

**Divergences from the RFC**
| # | RFC said | What shipped | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Quoting lives in a new `lib/csv.awk` as `csv_field`, loaded with `awk -f` (option A). | Quoting is inline in the awk program in `bin/invoices` (option B). `lib/` does not exist. | The Build pr-review flagged the separate file as a cut worth making, because the function has one caller. Lee agreed on 2026-11-06 (`plan.md`, "Departures from the plan"). The RFC's reason for A, reuse by other commands, is not needed yet. |
| 2 | The Fallback section expected option B only if `awk -f` with two program files behaved differently on the CI image. | Option B shipped, for the reason in row 1. The fallback condition never came up. | Same as row 1. |
| 3 | Two PRs: PR 1 for CSV output, PR 2 for quoting. | One PR, PR 42, holds both phases. | Recorded in `plan.md`, "Departures from the plan". It gives no reason. |
| 4 | Tickets REP-101 and REP-102. | No ticket is recorded in `sdlc.yaml`, `plan.md` or `review.md`. | Not recorded. Lee should say whether the tickets were opened, and if so, whether they should close against PR 42. |
| 5 | (Not in the RFC.) The plan has `tests/test_csv.sh` check that 2,000 invoices export in under 5 seconds (AC5). | `tests/test_csv.sh` has four checks, and none covers AC5. `verify.md` records a one-off manual run by the verifier (0.4 seconds). | Found by comparing the plan with the test file. No document records the reason. AC5 has no regression test. |

**Won't-fix findings** (`review.md`)
- `PERF|bin/invoices|csv|one awk process per call` (Low). The export reads the whole file in one awk process, and that is fast enough at 2,000 invoices.

**Gaps** (`verify.md`)
- Nothing checks an export larger than 2,000 invoices.

## Consequences
| What could break | Detection | Rollback |
|---|---|---|
| The table output changes | `tests/test_list.sh`. `verify.md` also records that the table output matched the base branch byte for byte. | Revert the merge commit of PR 42. |
| The CSV export slows down on a large file | Nothing automatic. AC5 was checked by hand once (see row 5). | Revert the merge commit of PR 42. |
| Another command needs the same quoting | A reviewer notices the duplicated awk code. | Pull the quoting out into `lib/csv.awk` then, as the RFC proposed. |

Supersedes: none.
Superseded by: none.

# ADR: `invoices list --csv` prints the listed invoices as CSV, with quoting inline in `bin/invoices`

Status: Accepted
Merged: 2026-11-08
PR: https://github.com/example/reports/pull/42
Approvers: Lee (approver of the RFC and every stage), Sam (owner)
Source RFC: `rfc.md`, approved 2026-11-04 and left unchanged
Supersedes: none
Superseded by: none

## Problem and goal
Finance copies `bin/invoices list` output into a spreadsheet by hand. The goal is one command that prints the listed invoices as CSV.

## Constraints
- POSIX shell and awk only, with no new dependency.
- The table output must not change.

## Options
| Option | Verdict |
|---|---|
| A. Quote fields in a new `lib/csv.awk` file that `bin/invoices` loads with `awk -f` | Chosen in the RFC, then dropped during Build. The function had one caller, so a separate file added nothing. |
| B. Quote fields inline in the awk program inside `bin/invoices` | Rejected in the RFC. Adopted during Build (see "As built"). |
| C. Pipe the table through `sed` to swap spaces for commas | Rejected. It breaks on customer names that contain spaces or commas. |

## Decision
`bin/invoices list --csv` runs the same `--status` filter as the table. It prints the header and each listed row as four comma-separated fields. The quoting lives inline in the CSV awk program in `bin/invoices`. For each field, the program wraps it in double quotes when it contains a comma or a double quote, and doubles each inner quote. There is no `lib/csv.awk` and no `csv_field` function. The table branch is unchanged.

The RFC's fallback (move the function inline if `awk -f` with two program files misbehaves on CI) became the shipped design, for a different reason: the extra file wasn't worth it.

## As built
Merged PRs:
- PR 42 (https://github.com/example/reports/pull/42), merged by Lee on 2026-11-08. It holds both phases.

Divergences from the RFC:
| # | RFC said | What shipped | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Quoting in a new `lib/csv.awk`, loaded with `awk -f`, as a `csv_field` function | Quoting inline in the awk program in `bin/invoices`. `lib/` doesn't exist. | The Build pr-review run flagged the separate file as a cut worth making, because the function has one caller. Lee agreed on 2026-11-06 (`plan.md`, "Departures"). The RFC's stated reuse by other commands didn't materialise. |
| 2 | Two phases in two PRs (PR 1 and PR 2) | Both phases in one PR, PR 42 | Recorded in `plan.md`, "Departures". The reason isn't written down there. |
| 3 | Tickets REP-101 and REP-102 | Not tracked in the repo. Nothing in the code or `review.md` references them. | Unknown. Lee should say whether they were closed against PR 42. |
| 4 | `plan.md` lists a test "2,000 invoices export in under 5 seconds" for AC5 | `tests/test_csv.sh` has four checks and no AC5 test. AC5 was proved once by hand: the verifier exported 2,000 invoices in 0.4 seconds (`verify.md`). | Found while comparing by hand. `verify.md` doesn't say the test is missing, and its AC5 row cites the verifier run only. Nothing in the suite guards the 5-second limit. |

Known gaps carried from `review.md` and `verify.md`:
- Won't fix: the export reads the whole file in one awk process (`PERF|bin/invoices|csv|one awk process per call`). Fast enough at 2,000 invoices.
- Nothing checks an export larger than 2,000 invoices.

## Consequences
| What could break | Detection | Rollback |
|---|---|---|
| The table output changes | `tests/test_list.sh`, and the verifier's byte-for-byte comparison with the base branch | Revert the merge commit of PR 42 |
| A quoting error corrupts a CSV field | `tests/test_csv.sh`, the comma and double-quote checks | Revert the merge commit of PR 42 |
| The export slows past the 5-second limit on a large file | Nothing automated (see divergence 4) | Revert the merge commit of PR 42 |
| Another command wants CSV quoting | Nobody notices until it's written | Extract the inline quoting into a shared file then. The RFC's option A is still open. |

# ADR: `bin/invoices list --csv` prints CSV, quoted by the awk program inside `bin/invoices`

Status: Accepted (draft, waiting for Lee's review)
Merged: 2026-11-08
PR: https://github.com/example/reports/pull/42
Approvers: Lee
Supersedes: none
Superseded by: none

## Problem and goal
Finance copies `bin/invoices list` output into a spreadsheet by hand. The goal is one command that prints the listed invoices as CSV.

## Constraints
- POSIX shell and awk only, with no new dependency.
- The table output must not change.

## Options
| Option | Verdict |
|---|---|
| A. Quote fields in a new `lib/csv.awk` file that `bin/invoices` loads with `awk -f` | Chosen. Other commands can reuse the quoting later. |
| B. Quote fields inline in the awk program inside `bin/invoices` | Rejected. It mixes the format with the listing. |
| C. Pipe the table through `sed` to swap spaces for commas | Rejected. It breaks on customer names that contain spaces or commas. |

The verdicts above are the RFC's, kept as the context of the decision. What shipped is option B. See "As built".

## Decision
`bin/invoices list --csv` runs the same filter as the table (`--status` applies to both formats), and prints each row as CSV from the awk program inside `bin/invoices`. For every field that contains a comma or a double quote, the program doubles each inner quote and wraps the field in double quotes. The header row goes through the same code. There is no `lib/csv.awk` and no `csv_field` function. Without `--csv`, the table code path is untouched.

## As built
Merged PRs:
- PR 42 (https://github.com/example/reports/pull/42), merged 2026-11-08 by Lee. It holds both phases.

Divergences from the RFC:
| RFC said | What shipped | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| Quoting lives in a new `lib/csv.awk`, in a `csv_field` function (option A) | Quoting is a loop inside the awk program in `bin/invoices` (option B) | The function has one caller. Build's pr-review flagged the separate file as a cut worth making, and Lee agreed on 2026-11-06 (`plan.md`, "Departures from the plan"). |
| Fallback: move the function inline if `awk -f` with two program files misbehaves on CI | The fallback's end state shipped, but it wasn't triggered | The reason was the single caller, not CI behaviour. |
| Two PRs: PR 1 for `--csv` output, PR 2 for quoting | One PR, 42, with both phases | Recorded in `plan.md`, "Departures from the plan". It gives no reason. |
| Tickets REP-101 and REP-102 | Not checked | Nothing in the repo or `docs/sdlc/csv-export/` records whether the tickets were filed or closed. Lee should confirm. |

Differences from the plan and the spec that Lee should know:
- `plan.md` maps AC5 to `tests/test_csv.sh` "2,000 invoices export in under 5 seconds". That test doesn't exist in `tests/test_csv.sh`, which has four checks. `verify.md` shows the verifier timing a 2,000-invoice export at 0.4 seconds by hand. AC5 was proved once and isn't a regression test.
- `verify.md` records that nothing checks an export larger than 2,000 invoices.
- `review.md` records one won't-fix finding: the export reads the whole file in one awk process (`PERF|bin/invoices|csv|one awk process per call`, Low). It is fast enough at 2,000 invoices.

## Consequences
| What could break | Detection | Rollback |
|---|---|---|
| The table output changes | `tests/test_list.sh`, and `verify.md` shows the output matches the base branch byte for byte | Revert the merge commit of PR 42 |
| A quoting bug in the inline awk loop | `tests/test_csv.sh` checks a comma and a double quote | Revert the merge commit of PR 42 |
| The export gets slow on a much larger file | Nothing. The 5-second check was manual, at 2,000 invoices. | Revert the merge commit of PR 42 |

Quoting can't be reused by another command without copying it. That was the benefit of option A, and it was given up.

# ADR: CSV export quoting lives inline in `bin/invoices`

Status: draft, awaiting review by Lee
Date: 2026-11-12
Feature: csv-export. PR: https://github.com/example/reports/pull/42, merged 2026-11-08.

## Context
Finance copied `bin/invoices list` output into a spreadsheet by hand every Monday. The service is POSIX shell and awk, with no room for new dependencies, and the table output had to stay byte for byte the same. The RFC (approved 2026-11-04) had to pick where the CSV quoting logic lives.

## Decision
`bin/invoices list --csv` ships with the quoting written inline in the awk program inside `bin/invoices`. A field that contains a comma or a double quote is wrapped in double quotes, and each inner quote is doubled. The same `--status` filter drives both the table and the CSV output. That is option B in the RFC.

## What shipped versus the RFC
| Topic | RFC | What shipped |
|---|---|---|
| Quoting location | Option A: `csv_field` in a new `lib/csv.awk`, loaded with `awk -f` (chosen) | Option B: inline in `bin/invoices`. `lib/csv.awk` doesn't exist. |
| Why the change | The RFC's fallback applied only if `awk -f` with two program files misbehaved on CI. | The Build-stage pr-review flagged the separate file as a cut worth making, because the function has one caller. Lee agreed on 2026-11-06. The fallback was never triggered. |
| PRs | Two: PR 1 for `--csv`, PR 2 for quoting | One: PR 42 carried both phases |
| Tickets | REP-101 and REP-102 proposed | Not checked in the repo. The RFC lists them, and nothing in the code or commits references them. |

The RFC rejected option B because "it mixes the format with the listing", and chose A so that "other commands can reuse the quoting later". Both points still stand. This decision accepts that cost, since there is one caller today.

## Consequences
- The quoting is a single line in the awk program. There's no second file to load, and no `awk -f` behaviour to check on the CI image.
- A second command that needs CSV output has to extract the quoting at that point, or copy it. Extraction would mean the `lib/csv.awk` design from the RFC, so that design remains the path to take.
- Header and data rows both go through the same quoting, so the header is quoted if a header field ever contains a comma or a quote.
- The table output is unchanged. `tests/test_list.sh` passes, and Test confirmed the bytes match the base branch.

## Verification state
`sh verify.sh` passes with 7 passed, 0 failed, re-run on 2026-11-12 on `sam/csv-export`.

| Criterion | Evidence |
|---|---|
| AC1, AC2, AC3 | Automated in `tests/test_csv.sh` |
| AC4 | Automated in `tests/test_list.sh` |
| AC5 (2,000 invoices in under 5 seconds) | Measured once by hand during Test, at 0.4 seconds. **No automated test exists.** |

Plan.md lists a `tests/test_csv.sh` test, "2,000 invoices export in under 5 seconds", for AC5. `tests/test_csv.sh` has four checks, and none of them is that test. The performance criterion is therefore unguarded against regressions.

## Open items
- AC5 has no automated test. Add one, or amend `plan.md` to say AC5 was verified by hand. This ADR doesn't decide which.
- Test noted that nothing checks an export larger than 2,000 invoices.
- Review finding `PERF|bin/invoices|csv|one awk process per call` was closed as won't fix, since it's fast enough at 2,000 invoices.

## Sources
`rfc.md`, `plan.md` (Departures from the plan), `verify.md`, `review.md`, `bin/invoices`, `tests/test_csv.sh`.

Compared by reading the RFC and the code, because EGM isn't installed on this machine.

# ADR: CSV export for the invoice list

Status: draft, for Lee's review
Date: 2026-11-12
PR: https://github.com/example/reports/pull/42, merged by Lee on 2026-11-08

## Context
Finance copied the output of `bin/invoices list` into a spreadsheet by hand every Monday. It took about 20 minutes, and rows got missed. The constraints were POSIX shell and awk only, no new dependency, and no change to the table output.

## Decision
`bin/invoices list --csv` prints the header `id,customer,amount,status` and then one CSV row per listed invoice. It uses the same `--status` filter as the table. A field that contains a comma or a double quote is wrapped in double quotes, and each inner double quote is doubled. The quoting is inline in the awk program in `bin/invoices`. The table output is the original awk program, unchanged, in the `else` branch.

## What shipped, compared with the RFC
The RFC (approved 2026-11-04) chose option A. The code differs from it in three ways.

| RFC said | What shipped | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Option A: a `csv_field` function in a new `lib/csv.awk`, loaded with `awk -f` | Option B: the quoting is inline in the awk program in `bin/invoices`. There is no `lib/` directory and no `csv_field` function. | The pr-review run for Build flagged the separate file as a cut worth making, because the function has one caller. Lee agreed on 2026-11-06. The RFC's fallback condition (`awk -f` misbehaving on the CI image) did not trigger. |
| Two PRs: PR 1 for output without quoting, PR 2 for quoting | One PR, PR 42, with both phases | Recorded in the plan's departures. The plan's phases still ran in order: output first, then quoting. |
| Tickets REP-101 (`--csv` output) and REP-102 (quoting) | Not checked. Nothing in the repo links either ticket to PR 42. | The repo has no ticket references beyond the RFC. Lee should confirm how the tickets are closed, and whether they merge into one. |

Option B was rejected in the RFC because it "mixes the format with the listing". The shipped code accepts that trade-off. The CSV branch is an `if` around a separate awk program, so the table path is untouched. Another command that needs CSV quoting would have to extract the logic at that point, and the RFC's reason for option A, reuse, is deferred until then.

## Consequences
- The change adds no dependency. It adds one option and one awk program to `bin/invoices`.
- The table output is unchanged. `tests/test_list.sh` passes, and the verifier confirmed the output matches the base branch byte for byte.
- The CSV and table paths repeat the row filter (`NR == 1 || status == "" || $4 == status`). A change to filtering has to be made in both places.
- Quoting handles commas and double quotes, as in the spec's R3. The code doesn't special-case embedded newlines, and the tab-separated source can't contain them.
- Each CSV export is one awk process over the file.

## Verification
`sh verify.sh` reports `7 passed, 0 failed`. I re-ran it on 2026-11-12 on this branch, and the result matches `verify.md`.

AC5 (a 2,000-invoice export in under 5 seconds) has one measurement behind it: the verifier saw 0.4 seconds in `verify.md`. **No automated test covers it.** The plan listed a `tests/test_csv.sh` check named "2,000 invoices export in under 5 seconds", but `tests/test_csv.sh` has four checks and that one is not among them. The plan's test table is wrong on this point.

## Open items
- Add the AC5 performance check, or amend the plan's test table to say AC5 was verified by hand. This needs Lee's decision.
- Nothing checks an export larger than 2,000 invoices. `review.md` recorded the one-process-per-call finding as won't fix.
- Confirm the status of REP-101 and REP-102.

## Rollback
Revert the merge commit of PR 42, as `review.md` says. Finance would lose `--csv` and go back to copying by hand.

# ADR: CSV export for the invoice list

Status: draft, for Lee's review
Date: 2026-11-12
Shipped: PR 42, merged by Lee on 2026-11-08
Feature artifacts: `intent.md`, `spec.md`, `rfc.md`, `plan.md`, `verify.md`, `review.md`

## Context
Finance copied the output of `bin/invoices list` into a spreadsheet by hand every Monday. It took about 20 minutes and rows got missed. The service is POSIX shell and awk, and the constraint was no new dependency.

## Decision
`bin/invoices list` has a `--csv` option. It prints the header `id,customer,amount,status` and one row per listed invoice. It combines with `--status`. A field that contains a comma or a double quote is wrapped in double quotes, and each inner double quote is doubled (RFC 4180). Without `--csv` the table output is unchanged.

The quoting is done inline in the awk program in `bin/invoices`. It does not live in a separate `lib/csv.awk`.

## What shipped
- `bin/invoices`: `--csv` is parsed in the option loop. With the flag set, one awk process filters the rows, quotes the fields and prints them. The table path is the original awk program.
- `tests/test_csv.sh`: four checks, for AC1, AC2 and the two AC3 rows.
- No new file under `lib/`, and no new dependency.
- Verify: `sh verify.sh` reports `7 passed, 0 failed` (4 in `test_csv.sh`, 3 in `test_list.sh`).

## Departures from the RFC
| RFC said | What shipped | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Option A: quoting in a new `lib/csv.awk`, loaded with `awk -f` | Option B: quoting inline in `bin/invoices` | The Build pr-review run flagged the separate file as a cut worth making, because `csv_field` had one caller. Lee agreed on 2026-11-06. |
| Two PRs: phase 1 unquoted output (REP-101), phase 2 quoting (REP-102) | One PR, 42, with both phases | Recorded in `plan.md` under "Departures from the plan". |
| Fallback: move the function inline if `awk -f` with two program files misbehaves on CI | Not triggered. The inline form was chosen on design grounds, not because of a CI failure | |

The rationale for rejecting option B in the RFC was that it mixes the format with the listing. That concern was set aside, not disproved. If another command needs CSV, the quoting will have to be extracted at that point.

## Consequences
- Finance gets the invoices as CSV with one command, which was the intent.
- The quoting is local to `bin/invoices` and can't be reused without extracting it. Option A's reuse benefit was given up.
- The export reads the file in one awk process. Review logged this as Low and marked it won't fix.
- Quoting is only in the CSV branch, so the table path doesn't depend on it. `tests/test_list.sh` guards the table output.

## Gaps and follow-ups
- **AC5 has no automated test.** The plan lists `tests/test_csv.sh` "2,000 invoices export in under 5 seconds", but that check isn't in the file. `verify.md` says the verifier timed a 2,000-invoice export by hand (0.4 seconds), and the `7 passed` count doesn't include it. I re-ran a 2,000-row export with commas in the customer names while writing this ADR, and it finished in about 0.01 seconds. The criterion is met, but nothing would catch a regression.
- Nothing checks an export larger than 2,000 invoices (carried over from `verify.md`).
- REP-101 and REP-102 were proposed as two tickets. They shipped in one PR, so the tickets may need closing together.

## Open questions
- Should the AC5 timing check be added to `tests/test_csv.sh`, or should the plan be amended to say it is a manual check?

# CSV export for the invoice list — lessons

Measured two weeks after release, 2026-11-12. Numbers come from Sam. No tool in the session reaches the signals.

## Outcome vs success criteria
| Criterion | Target | Observed | Met |
|---|---|---|---|
| Finance stops copying invoices by hand | All finance accounts | 9 of 11 stopped the Monday copy job. The 2 biggest accounts still copy by hand, because an export over 5,000 invoices takes more than 30 seconds and they give up. | No |
| An export of 2,000 invoices finishes in under 5 seconds | < 5 s | 3 s | Yes |

## What went wrong
- Intent — the performance criterion was set at 2,000 invoices, and nobody checked it against the size of the largest account. It passed, and the 2 biggest accounts still copy by hand.
- Test — `verify.md` measured 0.4 s for 2,000 invoices and notes "Nothing checks an export larger than 2,000 invoices". Production took 3 s for the same size. The cause is unknown.
- Ship — pr-review raised `PERF|bin/invoices|csv|one awk process per call`, and the disposition was "won't fix — it's fast enough at 2,000 invoices". That finding pointed at the cost that now blocks the 2 biggest accounts.

## Changes proposed
- [ ] Check: a test that exports 10,000 invoices and fails over a time limit. Sam sets the limit in `csv-export-large`.
- [ ] CLAUDE.md: none. This is the first occurrence, so the repeated-mistake rule doesn't apply yet.
- [ ] REVIEW.md: a `won't fix` on a PERF finding must cite a measurement at the size of the largest real user, not at the spec's test size. Lee signs off.
- [ ] Eval: none. The repo has no eval suite.

## Follow-ups
- `csv-export-large` (from: csv-export), owner Sam

# CSV export for the invoice list — lessons

Measured two weeks after release (2026-11-12).

## Outcome against the intent
| Success criterion | Result | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Finance stops copying invoices by hand | Partly met | 9 of the 11 finance accounts stopped the Monday copy job. The 2 biggest accounts still copy by hand. |
| An export of 2,000 invoices finishes in under 5 seconds | Met | A 2,000-invoice export takes 3 seconds in use. |

## What went wrong
- The 2 biggest accounts export more than 5,000 invoices, and that takes more than 30 seconds. They give up and copy by hand, so the problem the feature set out to remove is still there for the heaviest users.
- The spec set the size limit at 2,000 invoices, and nobody checked where the largest accounts sit. The intent never named the size of the biggest account.

## What we learned
- Time grows faster than the invoice count. 2,000 invoices take 3 seconds and 5,000 take more than 30. Linear growth would give about 8 seconds at 5,000. The cause is not yet known, and the follow-up has to find it.
- The 3 seconds in use are about 7 times the 0.4 seconds the verifier measured on a laptop (`verify.md`, AC5). A laptop timing does not predict timing where Finance runs it.
- The review finding `PERF|bin/invoices|csv|one awk process per call` was closed as won't fix because the export was fast enough at 2,000 invoices. That held only for the size we tested.
- `verify.md` listed the gap "Nothing checks an export larger than 2,000 invoices". It was recorded and not acted on, and it is the gap that mattered.
- `plan.md` maps AC5 to a test in `tests/test_csv.sh`, but that file has no timing check. AC5 passed on a manual run only, so nothing in `sh verify.sh` would catch a slowdown.

## Actions
| Action | Owner | Where |
|---|---|---|
| Make exports of 5,000 invoices and more finish in a time Finance will wait for | Sam | `docs/sdlc/csv-export-large` |
| Add an automated timing check at the larger size | Sam | `docs/sdlc/csv-export-large` |
| Get the invoice counts of the 2 biggest accounts before the next spec fixes a size target | Sam | `docs/sdlc/csv-export-large` |

## Review note
No Goldfish review was run for this close-out. EGM isn't installed on this machine.

# CSV export for the invoice list — lessons

Measured on 2026-11-12, two weeks after release.

## Outcome against the success criteria
| Criterion | Result |
|---|---|
| Finance stops copying invoices by hand | Partly met. 9 of the 11 finance accounts stopped the Monday copy job. The 2 biggest accounts still copy by hand. |
| An export of 2,000 invoices finishes in under 5 seconds | Met in the field: 3 seconds. |

The 2 accounts that didn't switch export more than 5,000 invoices. That takes more than 30 seconds, and they give up and copy by hand.

## What we learned
- **The success criterion named the wrong size.** The intent picked 2,000 invoices, and every later stage tested that number. Nobody asked how large the biggest accounts' exports are. The accounts with the most invoices were the ones with the most to gain, and the feature doesn't serve them.
- **The field was slower than the check.** `verify.md` records 0.4 seconds for 2,000 invoices on the verifier's machine. Finance measured 3 seconds. The gap is about 7x, and we don't know yet why.
- **Time grows faster than the row count.** Going from 2,000 to 5,000 invoices (2.5x the rows) took the export from 3 seconds to more than 30 (over 10x the time). This is the numbers Finance reported, not a profile.
- **We can't reproduce it locally.** On 2026-11-12 synthetic files of 2,000, 5,000 and 20,000 invoices, with commas and quotes in the customer names, each exported in under 1 second with the shipped `bin/invoices`. The cause may be in the data's shape or in where Finance runs the command. Don't assume it is the awk program.
- **The "won't fix" in `review.md` rested on the 2,000 figure.** The finding `PERF|bin/invoices|csv|one awk process per call` was closed as "fast enough at 2,000 invoices". The large-export follow-up reopens that question. The finding itself may or may not be the cause.
- **The AC5 timing test never landed.** `plan.md` puts "2,000 invoices export in under 5 seconds" in `tests/test_csv.sh`, but that file has four checks and none of them times an export. `verify.md` records a manual measurement instead. `verify.md` also lists "nothing checks an export larger than 2,000 invoices" as a gap, and that gap is the one that mattered.

## Follow-up
- `csv-export-large`, owned by Sam: `docs/sdlc/csv-export-large/`. It starts by reproducing the slowdown with Finance's real file shape and environment.

## Review of this stage
No Goldfish review was run. EGM isn't installed on the machine where this was written, so Lee is signing off on this document without it.

✗ fail follow-up-linked

pattern not found in file docs/sdlc/csv-export-large/sdlc.yaml

# CSV export for the invoice list — lessons

Measured two weeks after release (shipped 2026-11-08, measured 2026-11-12). Figures come from Sam's report. They aren't re-measured here.

## Outcome against the intent
| Success criterion | Result | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Finance stops copying invoices by hand | Partly met | 9 of 11 finance accounts stopped the Monday copy job. The 2 biggest accounts still copy by hand. |
| An export of 2,000 invoices finishes in under 5 seconds | Met | A 2,000-invoice export takes 3 seconds in use. |

## What we learned
- **The big accounts are the ones that matter, and the spec never covered them.** The 2 accounts that still copy by hand are the largest, so they carry most of the manual work the intent set out to remove. An export over 5,000 invoices takes more than 30 seconds, and they give up before it finishes.
- **Our size target came from the sample, not from finance's data.** AC5 and the non-functional requirement both stop at 2,000 invoices. Nobody asked how many invoices the largest accounts list.
- **The Test stage saw this gap and let it through.** `verify.md` lists "Nothing checks an export larger than 2,000 invoices" under Gaps. No criterion or follow-up picked it up.
- **The Ship stage closed the matching finding as won't fix.** The `PERF|bin/invoices|csv|one awk process per call` finding was dismissed with "it's fast enough at 2,000 invoices". That reasoning holds only up to the size we tested.
- **The laptop number didn't predict the production number.** The verifier measured 0.4 seconds for 2,000 invoices, and finance sees 3 seconds. The environments differ, so a laptop figure shouldn't stand in for a user's wait.
- **Time grows faster than size.** 2,000 invoices take 3 seconds, and 5,000 take more than 30. That is about 2.5 times the invoices and at least 10 times the wait. This is an inference from two data points. The cause is not established, and the code in `bin/invoices` makes one linear awk pass, so the cause may lie outside the awk program (the data source, the machine, or how finance runs the command).

## Follow-up
`csv-export-large`, owned by Sam. It starts at the intent stage, with the cause of the slowdown as its first open question.

## Not done
- No Goldfish review ran for this stage. EGM isn't installed on this machine, so the stage has had no review beyond Sam's.

## Sign-off

Close the feature out for Lee to sign off, and start the follow-up as csv-export-large with me, Sam, as its owner. EGM isn't installed on this machine, so there's no Goldfish review this time.

# CSV export for the invoice list — plan

Verify command: `sh verify.sh` (healthy output ends with `N passed, 0 failed`)

## Files to change
| File | Change |
|---|---|
| `bin/invoices` | Parse `--csv`. When set, print the header and the filtered rows as comma-separated values. In phase 2, run each field through `csv_field`. The table branch stays as it is. Update the usage comments. |
| `lib/csv.awk` (new, phase 2) | Holds `csv_field(s)`: wraps `s` in double quotes when it contains a comma or a double quote, and doubles each inner double quote. |
| `tests/test_list.sh` | Add the checks listed under Tests. Same `check` helper and `ok -` / `FAIL -` output that `verify.sh` counts. |

No new dependency, and no config option. `data/invoices.tsv` is not edited, because it already holds the comma and quote cases for AC3.

## Phases
| # | Phase | Covers | Depends on | First failing test | Runs in | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | CSV output | AC1, AC2, AC4, AC5, PR 1 in the RFC | — | `tests/test_list.sh::--csv prints the header and four rows` | main session | in_progress |
| 2 | Quoting | AC3, PR 2 in the RFC | 1 | `tests/test_list.sh::--csv quotes commas and doubles quotes` | main session | pending |

Status is `pending`, `in_progress` or `done`. One phase is in progress per session or worktree.

### Phase 1 — CSV output
Goal: `bin/invoices list --csv` prints `id,customer,amount,status` and then one row per listed invoice, with no quoting yet.

1. In the option loop of `bin/invoices`, add `--csv) csv=1; shift ;;`. Initialise `csv=0` next to `status=""`. `set -u` is on, so the variable must be initialised.
2. Pass `-v csv="$csv"` to the existing `awk` call. Keep the filter condition `NR == 1 || status == "" || $4 == status`. In the action, `if (csv) print $1 "," $2 "," $3 "," $4; else printf "%-8s ..."`. The `printf` line is not edited, so the table bytes can't drift (R4).
3. The header comes from line 1 of the data file, which already reads `id customer amount status`. It needs no special case.
4. `--csv` and `--status` may come in either order, because the loop handles each option on its own (R2).
5. Capture the table output before touching `bin/invoices` and write it into the AC4 test as the expected literal (see Tests). Do this in the first step of the phase, so the expected bytes come from the unchanged code.
6. AC5 is expected to pass with no extra work, because the program is a single awk pass. Its test is still written in this phase, so a slowdown shows up straight away.

Amounts stay strings. awk's `$3` is printed as it was read and never used in arithmetic, so `310.50` can't turn into `310.5` (CLAUDE.md convention).

### Phase 2 — Quoting
Goal: fields that contain a comma or a double quote are quoted as RFC 4180 describes.

1. Create `lib/csv.awk` with one function:
   ```awk
   function csv_field(s) {
     if (s ~ /[,"]/) { gsub(/"/, "\"\"", s); s = "\"" s "\"" }
     return s
   }
   ```
   Fields that contain neither character come back unchanged.
2. Load it in `bin/invoices`. An awk invocation can't mix `-f file` with an inline program text, so build the program text in a shell variable and concatenate: `awk ... "$(cat "$root/lib/csv.awk")
   $prog"`. The alternative is to move the whole program into a second `-f` file, which is a bigger change. Keep the `$(cat ...)` form unless it fails on the CI image, and then use the RFC's fallback of defining the function inline.
3. In the csv branch, print `csv_field($1) "," csv_field($2) "," csv_field($3) "," csv_field($4)`. The table branch is untouched.
4. The header goes through `csv_field` as well. It has no special characters, so it comes out unchanged.

## Todos
Only the phase in progress has todos. Build writes phase 2's todos when it starts that phase.

### Phase 1 — CSV output
- [ ] Capture the current `bin/invoices list` output and use it as the expected literal in the AC4 test
- [ ] Write the failing tests for AC1, AC2, AC4 and AC5, and watch the `--csv` ones fail
- [ ] Add `--csv` to `bin/invoices` until they pass
- [ ] Refactor, run `sh verify.sh`, and run pr-review

## Tests
All tests live in `tests/test_list.sh` and use `check`.

| Acceptance criterion | Test |
|---|---|
| AC1 | `tests/test_list.sh::--csv prints the header and four rows`. It compares the full output with `id,customer,amount,status` followed by the four sample rows. In phase 1 the AC3 rows are unquoted, and the phase 2 change updates this expectation to the quoted rows. |
| AC2 | `tests/test_list.sh::--status open --csv prints the header and the three open invoices`. It is also run as `--csv --status open`. Compare the full output, and not only the line count. |
| AC3 | `tests/test_list.sh::--csv quotes commas and doubles quotes`. It greps for the exact lines `INV-002,"Acme, Inc.",310.50,paid` and `INV-004,"Initech ""West""",4500.00,open`. |
| AC4 | `tests/test_list.sh::list without --csv is unchanged`. It compares the output with the byte-for-byte literal captured before the change. A line count would not catch a column-width change. |
| AC5 | `tests/test_list.sh::2000-invoice --csv export finishes in under 5 seconds`. It generates a 2,000-row TSV with awk into `$TMPDIR` (or `mktemp`), points `INVOICES_FILE` at it, and compares `date +%s` before and after. Remove the file afterwards. |

## Risks found while planning
- **Table output drift (the RFC's blast radius).** The existing test only counts lines, so it would miss a width or spacing change. The AC4 literal check closes that gap, and the table `printf` line is deliberately left unedited.
- **Riskiest step: loading `lib/csv.awk` (phase 2).** Awk can't take both `-f` and an inline program. The `$(cat ...)` approach works on POSIX awk, but it needs a check on the CI image's awk. The RFC's inline fallback applies if it fails.
- **Float handling.** Never use a number-context expression on `$3`. This would turn `310.50` into `310.5`, and AC3's `310.50` check would catch it.
- **Wall-clock timing test (AC5).** A 5-second bound with 1-second `date +%s` resolution can flake on a loaded machine. The expected run is well under a second, so the margin is wide.

## Size check (ponytail ladder)
- `lib/csv.awk`: this is the one new file. The spec doesn't need it, since quoting could be an inline function in `bin/invoices` (RFC option B), and that would be the smaller option. The approved RFC chose a separate file for reuse and rejected option B, and I kept that decision. **Lee, please confirm that you still want the file.** If you don't, phase 2 becomes an inline function and `lib/csv.awk` goes away.
- No new dependency, config option, class, or helper script. `awk`'s `gsub` and the shell's `date` cover everything.

## Review
EGM isn't installed on the machine that wrote this plan, so there was no Goldfish implementation-readiness review. `sdlc.yaml` should record the plan's `goldfish` tool as `"skipped: EGM not installed"`. Lee decides whether that is acceptable.

matched ^(?=[\s\S]*bin/invoices)(?=[\s\S]*tests/)

✓ pass no-code-yet ×2

pattern absent as expected

✓ pass not-self-approved ×3

found 3 matches (expected 3)

✓ pass nothing-on-main ×2

pattern absent as expected

✓ pass phases-start-with-a-test ×2

judge votes: PASS PASS PASS

| AC1 | `tests/test_list.sh::--csv prints the header and four rows`. It compares the full output with `id,customer,amount,status` followed by the four sample rows. In phase 1 the AC3 rows are unquoted, and the phase 2 change updates this expectation to the quoted rows. |
| AC2 | `tests/test_list.sh::--status open --csv prints the header and the three open invoices`. It is also run as `--csv --status open`. Compare the full output, and not only the line count. |
| AC3 | `tests/test_list.sh::--csv quotes commas and doubles quotes`. It greps for the exact lines `INV-002,"Acme, Inc.",310.50,paid` and `INV-004,"Initech ""West""",4500.00,open`. |
| AC4 | `tests/test_list.sh::list without --csv is unchanged`. It compares the output with the byte-for-byte literal captured before the change. A line count would not catch a column-width change. |
| AC5 | `tests/test_list.sh::2000-invoice --csv export finishes in under 5 seconds`. It generates a 2,000-row TSV with awk into `$TMPDIR` (or `mktemp`), points `INVOICES_FILE` at it, and compares `date +%s` before and after. Remove the file afterwards. |

## Risks found while planning
- **Table output drift (the RFC's blast radius).** The existing test only counts lines, so it would miss a width or spacing change. The AC4 literal check closes that gap, and the table `printf` line is deliberately left unedited.
- **Riskiest step: loading `lib/csv.awk` (phase 2).** Awk can't take both `-f` and an inline program. The `$(cat ...)` approach works on POSIX awk, but it needs a check on the CI image's awk. The RFC's inline fallback applies if it fails.
- **Float handling.** Never use a number-context expression on `$3`. This would turn `310.50` into `310.5`, and AC3's `310.50` check would catch it.
- **Wall-clock timing test (AC5).** A 5-second bound with 1-second `date +%s` resolution can flake on a loaded machine. The expected run is well under a second, so the margin is wide.

## Size check (ponytail ladder)
- `lib/csv.awk`: this is the one new file. The spec doesn't need it, since quoting could be an inline function in `bin/invoices` (RFC option B), and that would be the smaller option. The approved RFC chose a separate file for reuse and rejected option B, and I kept that decision. **Lee, please confirm that you still want the file.** If you don't, phase 2 becomes an inline function and `lib/csv.awk` goes away.
- No new dependency, config option, class, or helper script. `awk`'s `gsub` and the shell's `date` cover everything.

## Review
EGM isn't installed on the machine that wrote this plan, so there was no Goldfish implementation-readiness review. `sdlc.yaml` should record the plan's `goldfish` tool as `"skipped: EGM not installed"`. Lee decides whether that is acceptable.

# CSV export for the invoice list — plan

Verify command: `sh verify.sh` (healthy output ends with `N passed, 0 failed`)

## Files to change
| File | Change |
|---|---|
| `bin/invoices` | Accept `--csv` in the option loop. Pass `-v csv=1` to the existing awk call. In CSV mode, print the fields joined by commas instead of the `printf` table line. Phase 2 also loads `lib/csv.awk`. |
| `lib/csv.awk` (new, phase 2) | One function, `csv_field(s)`. It returns `s` wrapped in double quotes with each inner `"` doubled when `s` contains a comma or a double quote, and returns `s` unchanged otherwise. |
| `tests/test_csv.sh` (new) | Checks for AC1, AC2, AC3 and AC5, in the `check "name" 'cmd'` style of `tests/test_list.sh`. |
| `tests/test_list.sh` | Add the AC4 check that compares `bin/invoices list` with the golden file. |
| `tests/list.expected` (new) | Golden output of `bin/invoices list` on the sample data, captured from the base commit before any code change. |
| `bin/invoices` usage lines | Update the usage comment and the usage message to `[--status open|paid] [--csv]`. |

No other file changes. `verify.sh` already runs every `tests/test_*.sh`, so `tests/test_csv.sh` is picked up without edits.

## Phases
| # | Phase | Covers | Depends on | First failing test | Runs in | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | CSV output | R1, R2, R4, AC1, AC2, AC4, PR 1 in the RFC | — | `tests/test_csv.sh::--csv prints the header and four rows` | main session | in_progress |
| 2 | Quoting | R3, AC3, AC5, PR 2 in the RFC | 1 | `tests/test_csv.sh::--csv quotes fields with commas and quotes` | main session | pending |

Status is `pending`, `in_progress` or `done`. One phase is in progress per session or worktree.

### Phase 1 — CSV output
Ships as PR 1 (REP-101).

1. Before touching `bin/invoices`, run `bin/invoices list > tests/list.expected` on the base code. This is the byte-for-byte reference for AC4.
2. Add the AC4 check to `tests/test_list.sh`: `[ "$(bin/invoices list)" = "$(cat tests/list.expected)" ]`. It passes on the unchanged code, and it must keep passing through both phases.
3. Create `tests/test_csv.sh` with the AC1 and AC2 checks and watch them fail:
   - AC1: the first line of `bin/invoices list --csv` equals `id,customer,amount,status` and the output has 5 lines.
   - AC2: `bin/invoices list --status open --csv` has the same header and 4 lines, and the order of `--status` and `--csv` doesn't matter.
4. In `bin/invoices`, add `--csv) csv=1; shift ;;` to the option loop, with `csv=0` set before the loop. Pass `-v csv="$csv"` to awk. In the awk program, keep the filter condition as it is, and in the action use `if (csv) print $1 "," $2 "," $3 "," $4; else printf ...`. The data file's own first row is `id<TAB>customer<TAB>amount<TAB>status`, so the header row needs no special case.
5. Fields are not quoted yet. Phase 1 output for `INV-002` and `INV-004` is therefore not valid CSV. **Don't release PR 1 on its own to Finance**: merge PR 1 and PR 2 together, or keep `--csv` out of the usage text until PR 2 lands. Lee decides which (see Open questions).

### Phase 2 — Quoting
Ships as PR 2 (REP-102).

1. Add to `tests/test_csv.sh` and watch fail:
   - AC3: `bin/invoices list --csv` contains the exact lines `INV-002,"Acme, Inc.",310.50,paid` and `INV-004,"Initech ""West""",4500.00,open` (use `grep -Fx`).
   - AC5: generate a 2,000-invoice TSV in `$TMPDIR` with awk (same four columns, some names with commas and quotes), run `INVOICES_FILE=<file> bin/invoices list --csv >/dev/null`, and require that `date +%s` after minus before is under 5.
2. Create `lib/csv.awk` with `csv_field`. Test it through the AC3 check, not on its own.
3. In `bin/invoices`, load the function with `awk -f "$root/lib/csv.awk" -f /dev/stdin "$data" <<'EOF' ... EOF`, moving the existing awk program into the heredoc. POSIX awk can't mix `-f` with an inline program, so both programs must be files. Change the CSV print to `csv_field($1) "," csv_field($2) "," csv_field($3) "," csv_field($4)`.
4. Run the whole verify command. AC4 and the existing table checks must still pass, which proves moving the program into the heredoc didn't change the table output.
5. If `-f /dev/stdin` fails on the CI image, or two `-f` files behave differently there, use the RFC's fallback: define `csv_field` inline in the awk program (option B) and delete `lib/csv.awk`.

## Challenge: risk
- **Riskiest step: phase 2, step 3.** Moving the awk program into a heredoc touches the table path. AC4 is the guard, and the golden file is captured before any change, so the guard is not circular.
- **Awk portability.** macOS awk and the CI image's awk may differ on `-f /dev/stdin` and on a function defined in a separate file. Run the verify command on both. The RFC's fallback covers failure.
- **Amounts as strings.** `CLAUDE.md` says never parse amounts as floats. The code only concatenates fields and never does arithmetic on `$3`, so `310.50` stays `310.50`. A check in AC3 proves the trailing zero survives.
- **Not caught by the tests:** a customer name containing a newline or a tab (a tab can't occur in the TSV source, a newline can't occur on a TSV row). No change is needed. This is noted so nobody adds handling for it.
- **Timing check is coarse.** `date +%s` has one-second resolution, and the check can only fail when the run takes 5 seconds or more. A 2,000-row awk run should take well under 1 second, so the margin is large.

## Challenge: size (ponytail ladder)
| Addition | Result |
|---|---|
| `tests/list.expected` | Keep. AC4 requires comparing against bytes from before the change, and nothing in the repo holds them. |
| `tests/test_csv.sh` | Keep. The existing `test_list.sh` could hold the checks, but a feature file keeps the AC mapping readable. Smaller option: put them in `test_list.sh` instead. Lee's call. |
| `lib/csv.awk` | Kept because the approved RFC chose option A. Under the ladder it is a new file for one 5-line function that has a single caller. The RFC names option B (inline) as its own fallback. See Open questions. |
| `--csv` option, `-v csv` variable | Keep. R1 requires them. |
| New dependency or config option | None. |

- [ ] Add the AC4 check to `tests/test_list.sh` and confirm it passes on the unchanged code
- [ ] Write the failing tests `--csv prints the header and four rows` and `--status open --csv prints the header and three rows` in `tests/test_csv.sh` and watch them fail
- [ ] Implement `--csv` in `bin/invoices` until they pass, and update the usage text
- [ ] Refactor, run `sh verify.sh`, and run pr-review

## Open questions for Lee
1. **`lib/csv.awk` or inline?** The RFC chose a separate file so other commands can reuse the quoting, but no other command uses it today. Keeping the RFC's choice costs one file and the `-f /dev/stdin` heredoc. Inline (option B) is smaller. This plan follows the approved RFC unless you say otherwise.
2. **PR 1 on its own.** Between PR 1 and PR 2, `--csv` prints invalid CSV for names with commas or quotes. Should the two PRs merge back to back, or should PR 1 leave `--csv` out of the usage text?
3. **Goldfish review not run.** EGM isn't installed on this machine, so the `implementation` review didn't happen. `sdlc.yaml` records the `goldfish` tool as skipped for that reason. Please review the plan by hand, or rerun the review where EGM is available.

- [ ] Add the AC4 check to `tests/test_list.sh` and confirm it passes on the unchanged code
- [ ] Write the failing tests `--csv prints the header and four rows` and `--status open --csv prints the header and three rows` in `tests/test_csv.sh` and watch them fail
- [ ] Implement `--csv` in `bin/invoices` until they pass, and update the usage text
- [ ] Refactor, run `sh verify.sh`, and run pr-review

## Open questions for Lee
1. **`lib/csv.awk` or inline?** The RFC chose a separate file so other commands can reuse the quoting, but no other command uses it today. Keeping the RFC's choice costs one file and the `-f /dev/stdin` heredoc. Inline (option B) is smaller. This plan follows the approved RFC unless you say otherwise.
2. **PR 1 on its own.** Between PR 1 and PR 2, `--csv` prints invalid CSV for names with commas or quotes. Should the two PRs merge back to back, or should PR 1 leave `--csv` out of the usage text?
3. **Goldfish review not run.** EGM isn't installed on this machine, so the `implementation` review didn't happen. `sdlc.yaml` records the `goldfish` tool as skipped for that reason. Please review the plan by hand, or rerun the review where EGM is available.

# CSV export for the invoice list — plan

Verify command: `sh verify.sh` (healthy output ends with `N passed, 0 failed`)

## Files to change
| File | Change |
|---|---|
| `bin/invoices` | Parse `--csv`. When set, print the header `id,customer,amount,status` and one comma-separated row per listed invoice, using the same `--status` filter as the table. Without `--csv`, the table code path stays untouched. Update the usage comment and usage message. |
| `lib/csv.awk` (new, phase 2) | Defines `csv_field(s)`: returns `s` wrapped in double quotes, with each `"` doubled, when `s` contains `,` or `"`; otherwise returns `s`. The RFC chose a separate file (option A), so it stays. |
| `tests/test_list.sh` | Add the checks listed under Tests. Existing checks stay as they are. |

No other files change. No dependency is added.

## Phases
| # | Phase | Covers | Depends on | First failing test | Runs in | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | CSV output | AC1, AC2, AC4, PR 1 in the RFC | — | `tests/test_list.sh::--csv prints the header and four rows` | main session | in_progress |
| 2 | Quoting | AC3, AC5, PR 2 in the RFC | 1 | `tests/test_list.sh::--csv quotes commas and doubles quotes` | main session | pending |

Status is `pending`, `in_progress` or `done`. One phase is in progress per session or worktree.

### Phase 1 — CSV output
Goal: `bin/invoices list --csv` prints the header and one row per invoice, with no quoting yet.

1. In the option loop of `bin/invoices`, add `--csv) csv=1; shift ;;` and initialise `csv=0` next to `status=""`. Update the usage text to `invoices list [--status open|paid] [--csv]`.
2. Keep the existing `awk` call as the `csv=0` branch, byte for byte. Add the `csv=1` branch as a second `awk -F '\t' -v OFS=, -v status="$status"` call that has the same filter, `NR == 1 || status == "" || $4 == status`, and prints `$1,$2,$3,$4`. The input file's header row already carries the right column names, so `NR == 1` prints the CSV header with no special case.
3. Do not refactor the two branches into one program. The table branch must stay unchanged so AC4 holds by construction, and the test proves it.
4. Tests, written first:
   - `--csv prints the header and four rows`: the output's first line is `id,customer,amount,status`, and `wc -l` is 5 (AC1).
   - `--status open --csv keeps the header and the three open invoices`: `wc -l` is 4, and the output contains no `paid` line (AC2).
   - `list without --csv is unchanged`: compare `bin/invoices list` with a literal `printf` of the five table lines, taken from the output of the base commit before any edit. Capture that output first, with `git stash` or on a clean checkout, so the expected text is not copied from the new code (AC4).

Expected state after phase 1: INV-002 prints as `INV-002,Acme, Inc.,310.50,paid`, which is not valid CSV. That is why phase 2 exists, and the PR 1 description should say so. It is acceptable only because PR 1 and PR 2 are separate in the RFC. If they would ship apart, do not announce `--csv` until PR 2 lands.

### Phase 2 — Quoting
Goal: fields that contain a comma or a double quote are quoted as RFC 4180 describes.

1. Create `lib/csv.awk` with a single function:
   ```awk
   function csv_field(s) {
     if (s ~ /[,"]/) { gsub(/"/, "\"\"", s); return "\"" s "\"" }
     return s
   }
   ```
2. Load it in the `csv=1` branch of `bin/invoices`. POSIX awk takes the program either as `-f file` or as inline text, and mixing the two is not portable. So build one program text from the library plus the inline main program, and pass it as a single argument:
   ```sh
   prog="$(cat "$root/lib/csv.awk"; printf '%s\n' 'NR == 1 || status == "" || $4 == status { print csv_field($1), csv_field($2), csv_field($3), csv_field($4) }')"
   awk -F '\t' -v OFS=, -v status="$status" "$prog" "$data"
   ```
   Check it on the awk this machine has and on the CI image. This is the RFC's named fallback risk: if it behaves differently on CI, inline the function in `bin/invoices` (RFC option B) and delete `lib/csv.awk`. Raise that with Lee instead of deciding alone.
3. The print line in step 2 replaces the phase 1 `print $1,$2,$3,$4`.
4. Tests, written first:
   - `--csv quotes commas and doubles quotes`: the output contains the two exact lines `INV-002,"Acme, Inc.",310.50,paid` and `INV-004,"Initech ""West""",4500.00,open` (AC3). Use `grep -Fx`.
   - `--csv on 2,000 invoices finishes in under 5 seconds`: build a temporary file under `${TMPDIR:-/tmp}` with the header and 2,000 rows by running `awk` with a `BEGIN` block, including some rows that need quoting. Set `INVOICES_FILE` to it. Time the run with `date +%s` before and after, and require a difference below 5. Remove the file afterwards (AC5).
5. After AC3 passes, run the phase 1 checks again, because the print line changed.

## Risks
- **Riskiest step: loading `lib/csv.awk` in phase 2.** Shell and awk quoting around the library are where it can go wrong, and the behavior may differ between awk implementations (macOS awk, mawk, gawk). Check on at least the local awk and the CI image before opening PR 2.
- **Data that the tests won't catch:** an amount or customer with an embedded tab is already a problem in the TSV, and a customer name with a newline or a carriage return. Spec R3 covers only commas and quotes, so leave these alone, and don't add handling the spec doesn't ask for. If Lee wants them covered, that is a spec change.
- **Path assumptions:** `bin/invoices` uses `$root`. Reference `lib/csv.awk` through `$root` so the command works from any directory. Add no test for this, but run `cd /tmp && /path/to/bin/invoices list --csv` once by hand.
- **Timing test flakiness:** the 5-second limit is loose for 2,000 rows, and one awk pass takes a fraction of a second. A failure points at a real problem, such as a per-row `cat` or subshell. Don't shell out inside the row loop.

## Size check (ponytail ladder)
- `lib/csv.awk`: fails "does it need to exist?", because the inline function in `bin/invoices` would be smaller. It stays because the approved RFC chose option A so other commands can reuse it. Lee can overrule this at review, and the plan then drops the file and uses option B.
- No new options, config, dependencies or helpers beyond `--csv` and `csv_field`.
- The tests reuse `check` from `tests/lib.sh`. No new test file, and no fixture file: the expected table text is a literal in the test, and the 2,000-row file is generated and deleted.

## Todos
Only the phase that is in progress has todos here. Build writes the next phase's todos when it starts that phase.

# Plan: CSV export for the invoice list

Status: draft, for Lee's review
Implements: [rfc.md](rfc.md) (approved 2026-11-04) and [spec.md](spec.md) (R1-R4, AC1-AC5)

## Approach
The RFC picks option A: a `csv_field` function in a new `lib/csv.awk`, loaded by `bin/invoices` with `awk -f`. The work ships in two PRs, as the RFC's phases say. PR 1 adds `--csv` with no quoting. PR 2 adds the quoting.

All work stays in POSIX shell and awk, with no new dependency. Amounts stay decimal strings. The awk code never does arithmetic on `$3`, so `310.50` and `4500.00` keep their trailing zeros.

## What I found in the repo
- `bin/invoices` has one inline awk program. It prints the TSV header (`NR == 1`) and each row whose `$4` matches `--status`.
- The TSV header is already `id customer amount status`, so the CSV header is the same four names joined with commas.
- `tests/test_*.sh` use `check` from `tests/lib.sh`. `verify.sh` counts the `ok` and `FAIL` lines.
- The sample data has four invoices. Two of them, INV-002 (`Acme, Inc.`) and INV-004 (`Initech "West"`), cover AC3.
- `lib/` doesn't exist yet. PR 2 creates it.

## PR 1: `--csv` output (REP-101)
Covers R1, R2, R4 and AC1, AC2, AC4.

Steps:
1. **Capture the baseline first.** Before touching `bin/invoices`, save the current `bin/invoices list` output as `tests/fixtures/list.expected`. AC4 compares against that file byte for byte.
2. Add a `--csv` case to the option loop in `bin/invoices` that sets `csv=1`.
3. Pass `csv` into awk with `-v`. When `csv` is set, print `$1,$2,$3,$4` joined by commas, using the same filter as the table. When it isn't set, run the existing `printf` line unchanged.
4. Update the usage comments and usage message to mention `--csv`.
5. Add `tests/test_csv.sh`, in the same style as `test_list.sh`:
   - AC1: `list --csv` prints the header `id,customer,amount,status` and four data rows.
   - AC2: `list --status open --csv` prints the header and three rows. Run the options in both orders, since the option loop accepts either.
   - AC4: `bin/invoices list` matches `tests/fixtures/list.expected` (use `cmp`).

Done when `sh verify.sh` ends with `N passed, 0 failed`.

**Known gap in PR 1:** rows with a comma or a quote, such as INV-002 and INV-004, are not valid CSV until PR 2 merges. PR 1 must not be announced to Finance. Release the two PRs together, or merge PR 2 right after PR 1. I'd also keep PR 1's tests away from those two rows, because they would lock in the wrong output.

## PR 2: quoting (REP-102)
Covers R3 and AC3, AC5.

Steps:
1. Create `lib/csv.awk` with `csv_field(s)`. It returns `s` unchanged if it has no comma and no double quote. Otherwise it doubles each `"` with `gsub(/"/, "\"\"", s)` and wraps the result in double quotes.
2. Load it from `bin/invoices` with `-f`. The CSV row program has to move into a file too. POSIX awk can't take `-f` and inline program text together. I plan a second small file, `lib/csv_list.awk`, which holds the `--csv` row loop and calls `csv_field` on each field. The table path stays inline and untouched. **Lee, please confirm that one extra file is fine.** It's a small addition to the RFC's file list.
3. Resolve `lib/csv.awk` through the existing `$root`, so the command works from any directory.
4. Add to `tests/test_csv.sh`:
   - AC3: the export contains `INV-002,"Acme, Inc.",310.50,paid` and `INV-004,"Initech ""West""",4500.00,open`. Match them with `grep -Fx`.
   - AC5: build a 2,000-invoice file in `$TMPDIR` with awk. Include some comma and quote names, so the quoting path is timed. Run `INVOICES_FILE=... bin/invoices list --csv` and assert it finishes in under 5 seconds. Use `date +%s` before and after, since POSIX has no finer timer. Remove the file when done.
   - Re-run the PR 1 checks. They must still pass, and AC4 in particular.

Done when `sh verify.sh` shows 0 failed and the AC5 check passes.

## Spec traceability
| Req / AC | PR | Check |
|---|---|---|
| R1 / AC1 | 1 | `tests/test_csv.sh`: header and four rows |
| R2 / AC2 | 1 | `tests/test_csv.sh`: `--status open --csv`, both option orders |
| R3 / AC3 | 2 | `tests/test_csv.sh`: the two quoted rows |
| R4 / AC4 | 1 | `tests/test_csv.sh`: `cmp` against `tests/fixtures/list.expected`. `tests/test_list.sh` also still passes. |
| Non-functional / AC5 | 2 | `tests/test_csv.sh`: 2,000-invoice timing |

## Risks
| Risk | Mitigation |
|---|---|
| The table output changes (RFC blast radius) | The byte-for-byte fixture check in PR 1. The table `printf` isn't edited. Rollback is reverting the PR. |
| `awk -f` with two files behaves differently on the CI image (RFC fallback) | If the PR 2 checks fail on CI but pass locally, move `csv_field` inline in `bin/invoices` (option B). The tests don't change. |
| awk versions differ between macOS and Linux (BSD awk vs mawk/gawk) | Use only POSIX awk features: `gsub`, `index`, `printf`. No `gensub` and no `-v` array tricks. |
| The timing check is flaky on a loaded machine | 2,000 rows is tiny for awk, so there's a large margin against 5 seconds. Whole-second timing is enough. |
| Fields with embedded tabs or newlines | Not possible in TSV input, and the spec doesn't require handling them. |

## Out of scope
Scheduled exports and Excel format (from the intent). Other commands using `csv_field` is future work. This plan doesn't build it.

## Open questions
- Is a second file, `lib/csv_list.awk`, acceptable? (See PR 2, step 2.)

# Plan: CSV export for the invoice list

Status: draft, for Lee's review
Implements: [rfc.md](rfc.md) (approved 2026-11-04) and [spec.md](spec.md) (R1-R4, AC1-AC5)

## Approach
The RFC picks option A: a `csv_field` function in a new `lib/csv.awk`, loaded by `bin/invoices` with `awk -f`. The work ships in two PRs, as the RFC's phases say. PR 1 adds `--csv` with no quoting. PR 2 adds the quoting.

All work stays in POSIX shell and awk, with no new dependency. Amounts stay decimal strings. The awk code never does arithmetic on `$3`, so `310.50` and `4500.00` keep their trailing zeros.

## What I found in the repo
- `bin/invoices` has one inline awk program. It prints the TSV header (`NR == 1`) and each row whose `$4` matches `--status`.
- The TSV header is already `id customer amount status`, so the CSV header is the same four names joined with commas.
- `tests/test_*.sh` use `check` from `tests/lib.sh`. `verify.sh` counts the `ok` and `FAIL` lines.
- The sample data has four invoices. Two of them, INV-002 (`Acme, Inc.`) and INV-004 (`Initech "West"`), cover AC3.
- `lib/` doesn't exist yet. PR 2 creates it.

## PR 1: `--csv` output (REP-101)
Covers R1, R2, R4 and AC1, AC2, AC4.

Steps:
1. **Capture the baseline first.** Before touching `bin/invoices`, save the current `bin/invoices list` output as `tests/fixtures/list.expected`. AC4 compares against that file byte for byte.

**Known gap in PR 1:** rows with a comma or a quote, such as INV-002 and INV-004, are not valid CSV until PR 2 merges. PR 1 must not be announced to Finance. 2. Load it from `bin/invoices` with `-f`. The CSV row program has to move into a file too. POSIX awk can't take `-f` and inline program text together. # Plan: CSV export for the invoice list

Inputs: [intent](intent.md), [spec](spec.md), [rfc](rfc.md) (all approved by Lee).
Risk: low. Tickets: REP-101, REP-102.

## Approach
Follow the RFC: two PRs. PR 1 adds `--csv` with no quoting. PR 2 adds `lib/csv.awk` with `csv_field` and routes every CSV field through it. The table code path is not edited, so R4 holds by construction. A golden-file test checks it anyway.

Everything is POSIX shell and awk. Amounts are printed as the strings in the file and never used in arithmetic (CLAUDE.md convention).

## Spec coverage
| Requirement / AC | Delivered in | Test |
|---|---|---|
| R1 / AC1 | PR 1 | `tests/test_csv.sh`: header line and 4 rows |
| R2 / AC2 | PR 1 | `tests/test_csv.sh`: `--status open --csv` gives header + 3 rows |
| R3 / AC3 | PR 2 | `tests/test_csv.sh`: the two exact quoted rows |
| R4 / AC4 | PR 1 (guard), holds in PR 2 | `tests/test_list.sh`: `cmp` against golden files |
| Non-functional / AC5 | PR 1, re-run in PR 2 | `tests/test_csv.sh`: 2,000-row file under 5 s |

## PR 1: `--csv` output (REP-101)

1. **Golden baseline first.** Before touching `bin/invoices`, save the current output of `bin/invoices list` and `bin/invoices list --status open` to `tests/golden/list.txt` and `tests/golden/list-open.txt`. Add checks to `tests/test_list.sh` that compare with `cmp`. Don't compare through `$(...)`, which strips trailing newlines and would hide a byte difference. Run `sh verify.sh` to confirm they pass on unchanged code.
2. **Option parsing.** In `bin/invoices`, add `--csv) csv=1; shift ;;` to the `case`, defaulting `csv=0`. Update the usage line and the header comment to include `[--csv]`. Keep the existing unknown-option exit code 2.
3. **CSV branch.** After parsing, if `csv=1`, run a separate awk program with the same filter (`NR == 1 || status == "" || $4 == status`). It prints `$1,$2,$3,$4` joined with commas, unquoted. Otherwise run the existing table awk command exactly as it is.
4. **Tests.** Add `tests/test_csv.sh` (picked up by `verify.sh` through its `tests/test_*.sh` glob) with checks for AC1, AC2 and AC5.
   - AC5 builds a 2,000-row TSV in `$TMPDIR` using awk, and runs it through `INVOICES_FILE`.
   - Timing uses `date +%s` before and after and requires a difference under 5.
   - Don't add an AC3 check in this PR.
5. **Done when** `sh verify.sh` ends with `N passed, 0 failed` and the golden checks pass.

## PR 2: quoting (REP-102)

1. **Add `lib/csv.awk`.** It defines `csv_field(s)`: if `s` contains `,` or `"`, double each `"` with `gsub(/"/, "\"\"", s)` and return `"` s `"`. Otherwise return `s` unchanged. The function holds no state.
2. **Load it.** The CSV branch moves its awk program into a file, `lib/csv_list.awk`, and runs `awk -F '\t' -v status="$status" -f "$root/lib/csv.awk" -f "$root/lib/csv_list.awk" "$data"`. POSIX awk can't mix `-f` with inline program text, so the CSV program has to be a file. The table branch stays inline and untouched.
3. **Apply to every field** of every row, including the header, through `csv_field`.
4. **Tests.** Add the AC3 checks to `tests/test_csv.sh`: the output contains `INV-002,"Acme, Inc.",310.50,paid` and `INV-004,"Initech ""West""",4500.00,open` as exact lines. Use `grep -Fx`. Re-run AC1, AC2, AC4 and AC5 to show the function doesn't break them or slow the export.
5. **Done when** `sh verify.sh` ends with `N passed, 0 failed`.

## Fallback
If `awk -f` with two program files behaves differently on the CI image, apply the RFC fallback: define `csv_field` inline in the CSV program and drop `lib/csv.awk`. The tests don't change.

## Risks
| Risk | Mitigation |
|---|---|
| The table output changes | Golden `cmp` tests land before any code change. The table branch isn't edited. |
| PR 1 on its own gives wrong CSV for names with commas or quotes (Acme, Initech in the sample data) | Don't tell Finance about `--csv` until PR 2 has merged. Merge PR 2 promptly after PR 1. |
| The 2,000-row timing check is flaky on a loaded machine | `date +%s` has whole-second resolution, and the budget is 5 s. A single awk pass over 2,000 rows should take well under 1 s. |
| `set -eu` in `bin/invoices` and an unset `csv` variable | Initialise `csv=0` next to `status=""`. |

## Points for Lee
- **Deviation from the RFC:** the RFC says `bin/invoices` loads `lib/csv.awk` with `awk -f`. That needs the CSV program in a file too, so this plan adds `lib/csv_list.awk` in PR 2. The alternative is the RFC fallback (inline function). Say if you prefer that.
- **PR 1 ships unquoted CSV.** The plan follows the RFC phasing and relies on not announcing the option until PR 2 is merged.
- Newlines inside fields aren't handled. The data is one record per line, so the spec doesn't require it.

## Review status
No Goldfish review was run, because EGM isn't installed on this machine. This plan has only had author review. It's ready for Lee's review.
✓ pass names-real-files

matched ^(?=[\s\S]*bin/invoices)(?=[\s\S]*tests/)

✓ pass no-code-yet ×2

pattern absent as expected

✓ pass not-self-approved ×3

found 3 matches (expected 3)

✓ pass nothing-on-main ×2

pattern absent as expected

✗ fail phases-start-with-a-test ×2

5. **Done when** `sh verify.sh` ends with `N passed, 0 failed`.

## Fallback
If `awk -f` with two program files behaves differently on the CI image, apply the RFC fallback: define `csv_field` inline in the CSV program and drop `lib/csv.awk`. The tests don't change.

## Risks
| Risk | Mitigation |
|---|---|
| The table output changes | Golden `cmp` tests land before any code change. The table branch isn't edited. |
| PR 1 on its own gives wrong CSV for names with commas or quotes (Acme, Initech in the sample data) | Don't tell Finance about `--csv` until PR 2 has merged. Merge PR 2 promptly after PR 1. |
| The 2,000-row timing check is flaky on a loaded machine | `date +%s` has whole-second resolution, and the budget is 5 s. A single awk pass over 2,000 rows should take well under 1 s. |
| `set -eu` in `bin/invoices` and an unset `csv` variable | Initialise `csv=0` next to `status=""`. |

## Points for Lee
- **Deviation from the RFC:** the RFC says `bin/invoices` loads `lib/csv.awk` with `awk -f`. That needs the CSV program in a file too, so this plan adds `lib/csv_list.awk` in PR 2. The alternative is the RFC fallback (inline function). Say if you prefer that.
- **PR 1 ships unquoted CSV.** The plan follows the RFC phasing and relies on not announcing the option until PR 2 is merged.
- Newlines inside fields aren't handled. The data is one record per line, so the spec doesn't require it.

## Review status
No Goldfish review was run, because EGM isn't installed on this machine. This plan has only had author review. It's ready for Lee's review.

✗ fail todos-first-phase-only ×2
