# Plan: CSV export for the invoice list

Inputs: [intent](intent.md), [spec](spec.md), [rfc](rfc.md) (all approved by Lee).
Risk: low. Tickets: REP-101, REP-102.

## Approach
Follow the RFC: two PRs. PR 1 adds `--csv` with no quoting. PR 2 adds `lib/csv.awk` with `csv_field` and routes every CSV field through it. The table code path is not edited, so R4 holds by construction. A golden-file test checks it anyway.

Everything is POSIX shell and awk. Amounts are printed as the strings in the file and never used in arithmetic (CLAUDE.md convention).

## Spec coverage
| Requirement / AC | Delivered in | Test |
|---|---|---|
| R1 / AC1 | PR 1 | `tests/test_csv.sh`: header line and 4 rows |
| R2 / AC2 | PR 1 | `tests/test_csv.sh`: `--status open --csv` gives header + 3 rows |
| R3 / AC3 | PR 2 | `tests/test_csv.sh`: the two exact quoted rows |
| R4 / AC4 | PR 1 (guard), holds in PR 2 | `tests/test_list.sh`: `cmp` against golden files |
| Non-functional / AC5 | PR 1, re-run in PR 2 | `tests/test_csv.sh`: 2,000-row file under 5 s |

## PR 1: `--csv` output (REP-101)

1. **Golden baseline first.** Before touching `bin/invoices`, save the current output of `bin/invoices list` and `bin/invoices list --status open` to `tests/golden/list.txt` and `tests/golden/list-open.txt`. Add checks to `tests/test_list.sh` that compare with `cmp`. Don't compare through `$(...)`, which strips trailing newlines and would hide a byte difference. Run `sh verify.sh` to confirm they pass on unchanged code.
2. **Option parsing.** In `bin/invoices`, add `--csv) csv=1; shift ;;` to the `case`, defaulting `csv=0`. Update the usage line and the header comment to include `[--csv]`. Keep the existing unknown-option exit code 2.
3. **CSV branch.** After parsing, if `csv=1`, run a separate awk program with the same filter (`NR == 1 || status == "" || $4 == status`). It prints `$1,$2,$3,$4` joined with commas, unquoted. Otherwise run the existing table awk command exactly as it is.
4. **Tests.** Add `tests/test_csv.sh` (picked up by `verify.sh` through its `tests/test_*.sh` glob) with checks for AC1, AC2 and AC5.
   - AC5 builds a 2,000-row TSV in `$TMPDIR` using awk, and runs it through `INVOICES_FILE`.
   - Timing uses `date +%s` before and after and requires a difference under 5.
   - Don't add an AC3 check in this PR.
5. **Done when** `sh verify.sh` ends with `N passed, 0 failed` and the golden checks pass.

## PR 2: quoting (REP-102)

1. **Add `lib/csv.awk`.** It defines `csv_field(s)`: if `s` contains `,` or `"`, double each `"` with `gsub(/"/, "\"\"", s)` and return `"` s `"`. Otherwise return `s` unchanged. The function holds no state.
2. **Load it.** The CSV branch moves its awk program into a file, `lib/csv_list.awk`, and runs `awk -F '\t' -v status="$status" -f "$root/lib/csv.awk" -f "$root/lib/csv_list.awk" "$data"`. POSIX awk can't mix `-f` with inline program text, so the CSV program has to be a file. The table branch stays inline and untouched.
3. **Apply to every field** of every row, including the header, through `csv_field`.
4. **Tests.** Add the AC3 checks to `tests/test_csv.sh`: the output contains `INV-002,"Acme, Inc.",310.50,paid` and `INV-004,"Initech ""West""",4500.00,open` as exact lines. Use `grep -Fx`. Re-run AC1, AC2, AC4 and AC5 to show the function doesn't break them or slow the export.
5. **Done when** `sh verify.sh` ends with `N passed, 0 failed`.

## Fallback
If `awk -f` with two program files behaves differently on the CI image, apply the RFC fallback: define `csv_field` inline in the CSV program and drop `lib/csv.awk`. The tests don't change.

## Risks
| Risk | Mitigation |
|---|---|
| The table output changes | Golden `cmp` tests land before any code change. The table branch isn't edited. |
| PR 1 on its own gives wrong CSV for names with commas or quotes (Acme, Initech in the sample data) | Don't tell Finance about `--csv` until PR 2 has merged. Merge PR 2 promptly after PR 1. |
| The 2,000-row timing check is flaky on a loaded machine | `date +%s` has whole-second resolution, and the budget is 5 s. A single awk pass over 2,000 rows should take well under 1 s. |
| `set -eu` in `bin/invoices` and an unset `csv` variable | Initialise `csv=0` next to `status=""`. |

## Points for Lee
- **Deviation from the RFC:** the RFC says `bin/invoices` loads `lib/csv.awk` with `awk -f`. That needs the CSV program in a file too, so this plan adds `lib/csv_list.awk` in PR 2. The alternative is the RFC fallback (inline function). Say if you prefer that.
- **PR 1 ships unquoted CSV.** The plan follows the RFC phasing and relies on not announcing the option until PR 2 is merged.
- Newlines inside fields aren't handled. The data is one record per line, so the spec doesn't require it.

# Plan: CSV export for the invoice list

Spec: `spec.md` (approved 2026-11-03). RFC: `rfc.md` (approved 2026-11-04, option A).
Risk: low. Policies applied: none.

## Approach
Two PRs, in the order the RFC sets. Each PR is test-first: write the checks, watch them fail, then make them pass. `sh verify.sh` must end with `N passed, 0 failed` before each PR opens.

Constraints that apply to every step:
- POSIX shell and awk only. No new dependency.
- Amounts are decimal strings (CLAUDE.md). Print `$3` as-is. Never do arithmetic on it, and never use `$3+0`.
- Without `--csv`, the output of `list` stays byte-identical (R4).

## Findings from reading the code
1. **`bin/invoices` runs awk with an inline program.** awk cannot take both `-f file` and an inline program text. RFC option A (`awk -f lib/csv.awk`) therefore needs the existing listing program to move into a file too, or the two to be joined into one `-f` pair. PR 2 handles this. The table path must keep producing the same bytes when it does.
2. **R4 needs a baseline from before the change.** Nothing records the current table output today, so the baseline has to be captured from the unmodified script as the first step of PR 1.
3. **Header row.** The data file's header line is tab-separated `id customer amount status`. The existing awk prints it through the same filter as the rows (`NR == 1 || ...`). `--csv` can reuse that filter, and R1's header text matches the file's header fields exactly.
4. **`tests/lib.sh` appends to `.test-log` and `verify.sh` counts `^ok ` and `^FAIL ` lines.** New checks must go through `check` and print nothing else that starts with `ok ` or `FAIL `.

## PR 1: `--csv` output (REP-101)
Delivers R1, R2 and R4, plus the AC5 timing check. Quoting is not in this PR, so fields print joined by commas as they are.

Steps:
1. Capture the baseline. Run the unmodified `bin/invoices list` and `bin/invoices list --status open`, and save the output to `tests/fixtures/list.txt` and `tests/fixtures/list-open.txt`. This is the first commit of the PR, before any code change.
2. Add checks to `tests/test_list.sh` (or a new `tests/test_csv.sh`, which `verify.sh` picks up through its glob):
   - AC1: `list --csv` first line is `id,customer,amount,status`, and the output has 5 lines.
   - AC2: `list --status open --csv` has the header and 3 rows, and every row ends in `,open`.
   - AC4: `list` and `list --status open` match the two fixtures with `cmp`.
   - AC5: build a 2,000-invoice file in `$TMPDIR` with awk, point `INVOICES_FILE` at it, and require `list --csv` to finish in under 5 seconds (compare `date +%s` before and after).
   - Option handling: `list --csv --status open` and `list --status open --csv` give the same output, and an unknown option still exits 2.
3. Run `sh verify.sh` and confirm the new checks fail for the right reason (`--csv` is "unknown option").
4. Implement. Add a `--csv` case to the option loop that sets `csv=1`. In the awk call, pass `-v csv="$csv"` and choose the output format per row: `OFS=","` with `print $1,$2,$3,$4` when `csv` is set, and the existing `printf` otherwise. Update the usage comment and the usage message.
5. Run `sh verify.sh`. Open the PR with REP-101 in the title.

Done when: AC1, AC2, AC4 and AC5 pass, and the three existing checks still pass.

Known gap, deliberate: with the sample data, `INV-002` and `INV-004` come out unquoted in this PR. PR 1 must not be released to Finance on its own. Say so in the PR description.

## PR 2: quoting (REP-102)
Delivers R3. Builds on PR 1.

Steps:
1. Add the AC3 check first, with the two exact rows from the spec: `INV-002,"Acme, Inc.",310.50,paid` and `INV-004,"Initech ""West""",4500.00,open`. Check them with `grep -Fx`. Add one more check for a field with both a comma and a quote, and one for a plain field that must stay unquoted. Watch the checks fail.
2. Create `lib/csv.awk` with `csv_field(s)`: if `s` contains `,` or `"`, double each `"` and wrap the result in `"`, else return `s` unchanged. Use `gsub` and `index`/`~`, with no arithmetic on `s`.
3. Move the listing awk program out of `bin/invoices` into a file (for example `lib/list.awk`), and call `awk -f "$root/lib/csv.awk" -f "$root/lib/list.awk"`. Resolve both paths from `$root`, as the script already does for the data file. In CSV mode, print each field through `csv_field`.
4. Re-run the AC4 fixture checks. They prove the table output is unchanged after the program moved.
5. Re-run the AC5 timing check. Quoting adds a function call per field, so this is the check most likely to regress. If it is slow, test the cost on the 2,000-row file before changing anything.
6. Run `sh verify.sh`. Open the PR with REP-102 in the title.

Done when: AC3 passes, AC1, AC2, AC4 and AC5 still pass, and `verify.sh` is clean.

Fallback (from the RFC): if `awk -f` with two files behaves differently on the CI image, inline `csv_field` into the awk program in `bin/invoices` (option B). The same tests apply unchanged.

## Requirement and acceptance coverage
| Item | PR | Check |
|---|---|---|
| R1 / AC1 | 1 | header text and line count |
| R2 / AC2 | 1 | `--status open --csv` rows |
| R3 / AC3 | 2 | exact quoted rows, plus a comma-and-quote field and a plain field |
| R4 / AC4 | 1, re-run in 2 | `cmp` against fixtures captured before the change |
| AC5 | 1, re-run in 2 | 2,000-row file under 5 seconds |

## Risks
| Risk | Mitigation |
|---|---|
| The table output changes when the awk program moves to a file in PR 2 | AC4 fixtures, captured from the unmodified script |
| Two `-f` program files behave differently on CI | RFC fallback to option B |
| Quoting slows the 2,000-row export | AC5 re-run in PR 2 |
| PR 1 is released on its own with unquoted fields | The PR description says it is not releasable alone. Merge PR 2 before announcing to Finance. |

## Out of scope
Scheduled exports and Excel format (from the intent). Reusing `csv_field` in other commands (the RFC names it as a later benefit, not part of this feature).

## Open questions
- None.

Graders — what "good" means for this case

# CSV export for the invoice list — spec

Risk: low
Policies applied: none. No policy skills for security, compliance, brand, UX or API are loaded in this session. The repo's `CLAUDE.md` convention applies: amounts are decimal strings and are never parsed as floats.

## Summary
`bin/invoices list` gains a `--format csv` option that prints the listed invoices as RFC 4180 CSV on stdout, with a header row. It works together with `--status`. Without `--format`, the output stays exactly as it is today.

## Requirements
- **R1** `bin/invoices list --format csv` prints one header row (`id,customer,amount,status`) followed by one row per invoice in `data/invoices.tsv`, in file order, to stdout.
- **R2** `--format csv` combined with `--status open|paid` prints only the invoices with that status, plus the header row. The options work in either order.
- **R3** A field that contains a comma, a double quote or a newline is wrapped in double quotes, and each double quote inside it is doubled. Other fields are written unquoted.
- **R4** The `amount` field is copied through as the decimal string from the data file. It is never reformatted, rounded or padded.
- **R5** CSV output has no column padding and no leading or trailing spaces in fields.
- **R6** `--format table` is accepted and gives today's output. A `list` call without `--format` gives output identical to today's, byte for byte.
- **R7** An unknown `--format` value prints `unknown format: <value>` to stderr, prints nothing to stdout and exits 2.
- **R8** `--format` with no value prints a usage error to stderr and exits 2.
- **R9** The export reads the file `INVOICES_FILE` names when it is set, the same as `list` does today.
- **R10** The feature adds no dependency beyond POSIX shell and awk.

## Non-functional requirements
- Performance: an export of 2,000 invoices finishes in under 5 seconds on the machine finance uses. It does this in one pass over the data file.
- Correctness: the CSV opens in a spreadsheet with each invoice in its own row and each value in its own column, including rows whose customer name holds a comma or a quote.
- Compatibility: the output is UTF-8 and uses LF line endings.
- Data safety: the command only reads. It never writes or changes `data/invoices.tsv`.

## Acceptance criteria
| Id | Criterion | Covers |
|---|---|---|
| AC1 | On the sample data, `bin/invoices list --format csv` prints a header line and 4 invoice lines, in file order, with 4 columns each. | R1, R5 |
| AC2 | `bin/invoices list --format csv --status open` prints the header and INV-001, INV-003 and INV-004 only. `--status paid` prints the header and INV-002 only. Reversing the option order gives the same output. | R2 |
| AC3 | INV-002 prints as `INV-002,"Acme, Inc.",310.50,paid`. INV-004 prints as `INV-004,"Initech ""West""",4500.00,open`. | R3 |
| AC4 | Amounts `1200.00`, `310.50`, `89.99` and `4500.00` appear in the CSV exactly as written in the data file. A test file with the amount `0.10` and one with `100000000000000000.01` both come through unchanged. | R4 |
| AC5 | Parsing the sample CSV with a standard CSV reader (Python `csv` module, run in the test only) returns the same 4 rows and 4 columns as the data file. | R1, R3, R5 |
| AC6 | `bin/invoices list` and `bin/invoices list --status open` give output byte-identical to the output before this change, and `--format table` gives the same output as no `--format`. | R6 |
| AC7 | `--format xlsx` exits 2, writes `unknown format: xlsx` to stderr and writes nothing to stdout. `--format` with no value exits 2 with a usage message on stderr. | R7, R8 |
| AC8 | With `INVOICES_FILE` pointing at a 2,000-row file, `--format csv` finishes in under 5 seconds and prints 2,001 lines. | R9, NFR performance |
| AC9 | With `INVOICES_FILE` pointing at a file that holds only the header, `--format csv` prints the header line and nothing else, and exits 0. | R1, R9 |
| AC10 | `sh verify.sh` ends with `N passed, 0 failed`, and the diff adds no new dependency. | R10 |

## Risks and policy concerns
| Concern | Policy owner | Resolution |
|---|---|---|
| Risk level. The command reads invoice amounts and customer names. It is read-only, adds no new access and changes no data, so this spec sets Risk: low. Amounts are money data, and customer names may count as PII. | Lee (approver) | Open. Lee to confirm low risk. If Lee rates it high, a tech lead must also approve and the RFC can't be skipped. |
| Spreadsheet formula injection. A customer name that starts with `=`, `+`, `-` or `@` can run as a formula when a spreadsheet opens the CSV. | Lee (no security policy owner is named in the repo) | Open. The spec doesn't escape these values, because R3 and R4 say fields are written as they are. Lee to decide whether to add a requirement. |
| Quoting bugs. Customer names with commas and quotes already exist in the sample data, so naive splitting breaks rows. | Sam (owner) | Resolved. R3 and AC3 to AC5 cover it. |
| Float handling of amounts. | Repo convention in `CLAUDE.md` | Resolved. R4 and AC4 require decimal strings to pass through unchanged. |

## Open questions
- Does finance want the flag spelled `--format csv`, or a separate `export` command? This spec assumes `--format csv` because it composes with `--status`. — owner: Sam
- Does the finance team's spreadsheet need CRLF line endings or a UTF-8 byte-order mark to open the file cleanly? This spec assumes LF and no byte-order mark. — owner: Sam
- Is the risk level low, and does the formula-injection concern need a requirement? — owner: Lee
- The intent was approved without an `egm/concepts_brief.md`, so this spec was written from `intent.md` alone. — owner: Sam

# CSV export for the invoice list — spec

Risk: low
Policies applied: none. No policy skills for security, compliance, brand, UX or API are loaded in this session. The repo's `CLAUDE.md` convention applies: amounts are decimal strings and are never parsed as floats.

## Summary
`bin/invoices list` gains a `--format csv` option that prints the listed invoices as RFC 4180 CSV on stdout, with a header row. It works together with `--status`. Without `--format`, the output stays exactly as it is today.

## Requirements
- **R1** `bin/invoices list --format csv` prints one header row (`id,customer,amount,status`) followed by one row per invoice in `data/invoices.tsv`, in file order, to stdout.
- **R2** `--format csv` combined with `--status open|paid` prints only the invoices with that status, plus the header row. The options work in either order.
- **R3** A field that contains a comma, a double quote or a newline is wrapped in double quotes, and each double quote inside it is doubled. Other fields are written unquoted.
- **R4** The `amount` field is copied through as the decimal string from the data file. It is never reformatted, rounded or padded.
- **R5** CSV output has no column padding and no leading or trailing spaces in fields.
- **R6** `--format table` is accepted and gives today's output. A `list` call without `--format` gives output identical to today's, byte for byte.
- **R7** An unknown `--format` value prints `unknown format: <value>` to stderr, prints nothing to stdout and exits 2.
- **R8** `--format` with no value prints a usage error to stderr and exits 2.
- **R9** The export reads the file `INVOICES_FILE` names when it is set, the same as `list` does today.
- **R10** The feature adds no dependency beyond POSIX shell and awk.

## Non-functional requirements
- Performance: an export of 2,000 invoices finishes in under 5 seconds on the machine finance uses. It does this in one pass over the data file.
- Correctness: the CSV opens in a spreadsheet with each invoice in its own row and each value in its own column, including rows whose customer name holds a comma or a quote.
- Compatibility: the output is UTF-8 and uses LF line endings.
- Data safety: the command only reads. It never writes or changes `data/invoices.tsv`.

## Acceptance criteria
| Id | Criterion | Covers |
|---|---|---|
| AC1 | On the sample data, `bin/invoices list --format csv` prints a header line and 4 invoice lines, in file order, with 4 columns each. | R1, R5 |
| AC2 | `bin/invoices list --format csv --status open` prints the header and INV-001, INV-003 and INV-004 only. `--status paid` prints the header and INV-002 only. Reversing the option order gives the same output. | R2 |
| AC3 | INV-002 prints as `INV-002,"Acme, Inc.",310.50,paid`. INV-004 prints as `INV-004,"Initech ""West""",4500.00,open`. | R3 |
| AC4 | Amounts `1200.00`, `310.50`, `89.99` and `4500.00` appear in the CSV exactly as written in the data file. A test file with the amount `0.10` and one with `100000000000000000.01` both come through unchanged. | R4 |
| AC5 | Parsing the sample CSV with a standard CSV reader (Python `csv` module, run in the test only) returns the same 4 rows and 4 columns as the data file. | R1, R3, R5 |
| AC6 | `bin/invoices list` and `bin/invoices list --status open` give output byte-identical to the output before this change, and `--format table` gives the same output as no `--format`. | R6 |
| AC7 | `--format xlsx` exits 2, writes `unknown format: xlsx` to stderr and writes nothing to stdout. `--format` with no value exits 2 with a usage message on stderr. | R7, R8 |
| AC8 | With `INVOICES_FILE` pointing at a 2,000-row file, `--format csv` finishes in under 5 seconds and prints 2,001 lines. | R9, NFR performance |
| AC9 | With `INVOICES_FILE` pointing at a file that holds only the header, `--format csv` prints the header line and nothing else, and exits 0. | R1, R9 |
| AC10 | `sh verify.sh` ends with `N passed, 0 failed`, and the diff adds no new dependency. | R10 |

# CSV export for the invoice list — spec

Risk: low
Policies applied: none. The session has no security, compliance, brand, UX or API policy skills.

## Summary
`bin/invoices list` gains a `--format csv` option. It prints the same invoices the table would show as RFC 4180 CSV that a spreadsheet opens directly. It works with `--status`, and the default table output doesn't change.

## Requirements
- **R1** `bin/invoices list --format csv` prints one header row (`id,customer,amount,status`) followed by one row per invoice, with no column padding.
- **R2** The CSV rows are exactly the rows the table output would show for the same arguments. `--status open|paid` filters CSV output the same way it filters the table.
- **R3** A field that contains a comma, a double quote or a line break is wrapped in double quotes, and each double quote inside it is doubled. Example: `Acme, Inc.` becomes `"Acme, Inc."` and `Initech "West"` becomes `"Initech ""West"""`.
- **R4** The `amount` field is copied through as the decimal string in the data file. It is never parsed as a float or reformatted (CLAUDE.md convention).
- **R5** Records end with CRLF, as RFC 4180 specifies.
- **R6** `--format` accepts `csv` and `table`. `table` and omitting the option both give today's output, byte for byte. Any other value prints an error to stderr and exits 2, like other bad arguments.
- **R7** CSV output goes to stdout and nothing else goes there. Finance saves it with a shell redirect (`> invoices.csv`). The command writes no files itself.
- **R8** The export uses only POSIX shell and awk, and adds no dependency.
- **R9** `INVOICES_FILE` still selects the data file for CSV output.

## Non-functional requirements
- Performance: a CSV export of 2,000 invoices finishes in under 5 seconds (intent success criterion).
- Compatibility: the CSV opens in Excel and Google Sheets with the columns split correctly and the quoted fields intact.
- Portability: the awk code runs on the POSIX awk that ships with macOS and on common Linux awk implementations.

## Acceptance criteria
| Id | Criterion | Covers |
|---|---|---|
| AC1 | With the sample data, `bin/invoices list --format csv` prints the header `id,customer,amount,status` and four invoice rows, and every row has four fields when parsed as CSV. | R1, R2 |
| AC2 | `--format csv --status open` prints the header and the three open invoices. `--status paid` prints the header and one row. | R2 |
| AC3 | The row for `Acme, Inc.` reads `INV-002,"Acme, Inc.",310.50,paid` and parses back to the customer `Acme, Inc.`. The row for `Initech "West"` reads `INV-004,"Initech ""West""",4500.00,open` and parses back to `Initech "West"`. | R3 |
| AC4 | A data file with a customer name that contains a line break exports as one quoted field, and the output parses back to the original name. | R3 |
| AC5 | Every amount in the CSV is character-for-character equal to the amount in the data file, including trailing zeros (`1200.00`) and a leading minus sign. | R4 |
| AC6 | Every line of the CSV output ends with CRLF. | R5 |
| AC7 | `bin/invoices list` and `bin/invoices list --format table` produce identical output to the output before this change, for both the unfiltered and the `--status` cases. | R6 |
| AC8 | `bin/invoices list --format xml` exits 2, prints a message to stderr and prints nothing to stdout. `--format` with no value also exits 2. | R6 |
| AC9 | Redirecting CSV output to a file gives a file that contains only the CSV, and no file other than the redirect target is created. | R7 |
| AC10 | With `INVOICES_FILE` set to a 2,000-invoice file, `--format csv` finishes in under 5 seconds. | NFR performance |
| AC11 | `INVOICES_FILE` pointing at another file exports that file's rows. | R9 |
| AC12 | A repo search finds no new dependency or non-POSIX tool in `bin/invoices`, and `sh verify.sh` ends with `N passed, 0 failed`. | R8 |

## Risks and policy concerns
| Concern | Policy owner | Resolution |
|---|---|---|
| Spreadsheet formula injection: a customer name that starts with `=`, `+`, `-` or `@` can run as a formula when finance opens the CSV. Prefixing every such field would also corrupt negative amounts such as `-50.00`. | Lee (approver) | Open. See the first open question. |
| Risk level. The export is read-only and uses data the list command already prints. Amounts are financial data and customer names may identify people, so Lee should confirm that Risk: low is right. If it is high, the tech lead becomes an approver and the RFC can't be skipped. | Lee | Open. See the second open question. |
| The command exposes no new data, adds no access path and adds no network call. | n/a | Nothing to resolve. |

## Open questions
- Should customer names that start with `=`, `+`, `-` or `@` be neutralized, for example with a leading apostrophe or space, in the `customer` field only and never in `amount`? Without this, such a name is a formula injection risk when finance opens the file. — owner: Lee
- Does Lee agree that this is a low-risk change? — owner: Lee
- Is the option name `--format csv` acceptable, or does finance want a separate subcommand such as `export`? The spec assumes `--format csv` because it composes with `--status` and leaves the default output unchanged. — owner: Sam

# CSV export for the invoice list — spec

Risk: low
Policies applied: none. No security, compliance, brand, UX or API policy skill is loaded in this session. `CLAUDE.md`'s convention that amounts are decimal strings, never floats, is applied (R4).

## Summary
`bin/invoices list` gains a CSV output mode. Finance runs one command and gets the listed invoices as RFC 4180 CSV that opens correctly in a spreadsheet. The existing table output and the `--status` filter keep working as they do today.

## Requirements
- **R1** The system prints the invoice list as CSV when `bin/invoices list` is run with `--format csv`. Without `--format`, or with `--format table`, the output is the current table, byte for byte.
- **R2** The first CSV line is the header `id,customer,amount,status`. Each later line is one invoice, in the same order as the source file.
- **R3** The system quotes a field when it contains a comma, a double quote or a newline. It wraps the field in double quotes and doubles each embedded double quote. It leaves other fields unquoted.
- **R4** The system prints the amount exactly as it appears in the source file, as a decimal string with no float conversion, rounding or reformatting.
- **R5** `--status open|paid` works with `--format csv` and keeps the header line. The header is printed even when no invoice matches.
- **R6** The system uses `INVOICES_FILE` as the source in CSV mode, as it does today.
- **R7** `--format` with any value other than `csv` or `table` prints a usage error to stderr, writes nothing to stdout and exits 2. `--format` with no value does the same.
- **R8** CSV goes to stdout only, with no extra lines, and lines end in `\n`. Errors go to stderr.

## Non-functional requirements
- Performance: exporting 2,000 invoices takes under 5 seconds wall-clock on the finance team's machine (from the intent).
- Dependencies: none new. The implementation is POSIX `sh` and `awk` only.
- Compatibility: the exit codes and error messages for `list` and `--status` stay as they are today.
- Security: the command is read-only and never writes to the data file.

## Acceptance criteria
| Id | Criterion | Covers |
|---|---|---|
| AC1 | `bin/invoices list --format csv` on `data/invoices.tsv` prints the header `id,customer,amount,status`, then one line per invoice in file order. | R1, R2 |
| AC2 | `INV-002` appears as `INV-002,"Acme, Inc.",310.50,paid`. | R3 |
| AC3 | `INV-004` appears as `INV-004,"Initech ""West""",4500.00,open`. | R3 |
| AC4 | A customer name containing a newline is quoted and kept as one CSV record. | R3 |
| AC5 | An amount such as `1200.00` or `0.10` comes out unchanged, including the trailing zeros. | R4 |
| AC6 | `list --format csv --status paid` prints the header and only paid invoices. A status with no matches prints the header only. | R5 |
| AC7 | With `INVOICES_FILE` set to another file, CSV mode reads that file. | R6 |
| AC8 | `list` and `list --format table` produce output identical to the output before this change. | R1 |
| AC9 | `--format xlsx` and a trailing `--format` each print usage to stderr, print nothing to stdout and exit 2. | R7 |
| AC10 | With stderr discarded, stdout holds only CSV lines. | R8 |
| AC11 | A generated file of 2,000 invoices exports in under 5 seconds. | NFR performance |
| AC12 | `sh verify.sh` ends with `N passed, 0 failed`, and the new tests are among the passes. | R1–R8 |

## Risks and policy concerns
| Concern | Policy owner | Resolution |
|---|---|---|
| Customer names and invoice amounts leave the terminal as a file that can be shared. | Lee (approver). No policy skill is loaded and the spec names no other owner. | Open. The export shows the same fields `list` already shows to the same users, so Sam rates the risk low. Lee to confirm at approval. |
| Spreadsheet formula injection: a customer name starting with `=`, `+`, `-` or `@` may run as a formula when opened. | Lee (approver) | Open. The spec doesn't neutralize such values, because R3 and R4 require the data to come out unchanged. Lee to decide whether to accept this for finance's internal data. |
| Names with commas and quotes are in the real data, so naive comma joining would corrupt rows. | Sam | Resolved. R3 and AC2–AC4 cover it. |

## Open questions
- Is the flag name `--format csv` acceptable, or does Lee prefer a separate `export` subcommand? The intent says only "one command". — owner: Lee
- Should the formula-injection concern above be accepted as is? — owner: Lee
- Does the 5-second limit apply on the finance team's machine or on CI? AC11 assumes the finance machine. — owner: Sam

- **R8** CSV goes to stdout only, with no extra lines, and lines end in `\n`. Errors go to stderr.

## Non-functional requirements
- Performance: exporting 2,000 invoices takes under 5 seconds wall-clock on the finance team's machine (from the intent).
- Dependencies: none new. The implementation is POSIX `sh` and `awk` only.
- Compatibility: the exit codes and error messages for `list` and `--status` stay as they are today.
- Security: the command is read-only and never writes to the data file.

## Acceptance criteria
| Id | Criterion | Covers |
|---|---|---|
| AC1 | `bin/invoices list --format csv` on `data/invoices.tsv` prints the header `id,customer,amount,status`, then one line per invoice in file order. | R1, R2 |
| AC2 | `INV-002` appears as `INV-002,"Acme, Inc.",310.50,paid`. | R3 |
| AC3 | `INV-004` appears as `INV-004,"Initech ""West""",4500.00,open`. | R3 |
| AC4 | A customer name containing a newline is quoted and kept as one CSV record. | R3 |
| AC5 | An amount such as `1200.00` or `0.10` comes out unchanged, including the trailing zeros. | R4 |
| AC6 | `list --format csv --status paid` prints the header and only paid invoices. A status with no matches prints the header only. | R5 |
| AC7 | With `INVOICES_FILE` set to another file, CSV mode reads that file. | R6 |
| AC8 | `list` and `list --format table` produce output identical to the output before this change. | R1 |
| AC9 | `--format xlsx` and a trailing `--format` each print usage to stderr, print nothing to stdout and exit 2. | R7 |
| AC10 | With stderr discarded, stdout holds only CSV lines. | R8 |
| AC11 | A generated file of 2,000 invoices exports in under 5 seconds. | NFR performance |
| AC12 | `sh verify.sh` ends with `N passed, 0 failed`, and the new tests are among the passes. | R1–R8 |

## Risks and policy concerns
| Concern | Policy owner | Resolution |
|---|---|---|
| Customer names and invoice amounts leave the terminal as a file that can be shared. | Lee (approver). No policy skill is loaded and the spec names no other owner. | Open. The export shows the same fields `list` already shows to the same users, so Sam rates the risk low. Lee to confirm at approval. |
| Spreadsheet formula injection: a customer name starting with `=`, `+`, `-` or `@` may run as a formula when opened. | Lee (approver) | Open. The spec doesn't neutralize such values, because R3 and R4 require the data to come out unchanged. Lee to decide whether to accept this for finance's internal data. |
| Names with commas and quotes are in the real data, so naive comma joining would corrupt rows. | Sam | Resolved. R3 and AC2–AC4 cover it. |

## Open questions
- Is the flag name `--format csv` acceptable, or does Lee prefer a separate `export` subcommand? The intent says only "one command". — owner: Lee
- Should the formula-injection concern above be accepted as is? — owner: Lee
- Does the 5-second limit apply on the finance team's machine or on CI? AC11 assumes the finance machine. — owner: Sam

# CSV export for the invoice list: spec

Implements `intent.md` (approved by Lee, 2026-11-02).

## Behaviour

Add a subcommand to `bin/invoices`:

    bin/invoices export [--status open|paid]

- Writes CSV to stdout. Finance redirects it to a file (`bin/invoices export > invoices.csv`) and opens that in a spreadsheet.
- Reads the same source as `list`: `data/invoices.tsv`, or the file named by `INVOICES_FILE`.
- `--status` filters exactly as it does for `list`. Without it, every invoice is exported.
- The first row is always the header: `id,customer,amount,status`.
- One CSV row per invoice, in file order, with the same four columns as the source.
- `list` is unchanged.

### Format rules
- Fields are separated by `,`. Rows end with `\n`.
- A field is wrapped in double quotes when it contains a comma, a double quote, or leading/trailing spaces. Inside a quoted field, each `"` is doubled. Other fields are written bare. (RFC 4180 quoting.)
- Amounts are copied through as the original decimal strings. They are never parsed as numbers, so `1200.00` stays `1200.00` (CLAUDE.md convention).
- Unlike `list`, no column padding is added.

### Errors
- An unknown option exits 2 with a message on stderr, as `list` does.
- An unreadable data file exits non-zero with nothing on stdout beyond what awk already wrote. No new error handling is added beyond what `list` has.
- A filter that matches nothing prints the header only and exits 0.

## Design
- Implemented in `bin/invoices` as a second branch of the existing argument handling, sharing the `--status` parsing with `list`.
- A single awk pass over the file. No per-row process spawning, which keeps 2,000 invoices well inside the 5 second budget.
- POSIX shell and awk only. No new dependencies.

## Acceptance criteria

Each one becomes a check in `tests/test_export.sh`, run by `sh verify.sh`.

| # | Criterion | Check |
|---|-----------|-------|
| 1 | `export` prints the header and all four sample invoices | Output is 5 lines; line 1 is `id,customer,amount,status` |
| 2 | A customer containing a comma is quoted | `INV-002` line is `INV-002,"Acme, Inc.",310.50,paid` |
| 3 | A customer containing double quotes is quoted and the quotes doubled | `INV-004` line is `INV-004,"Initech ""West""",4500.00,open` |
| 4 | Amounts are unchanged | `INV-001` line ends `1200.00,open`; `INV-003` has `89.99` |
| 5 | `--status open` keeps the header and the three open invoices | 4 lines, none with `paid` |
| 6 | `--status paid` keeps the header and one invoice | 2 lines |
| 7 | A filter with no matches prints the header only and exits 0 | 1 line, exit 0 |
| 8 | An unknown option exits 2 | Exit code is 2 |
| 9 | `INVOICES_FILE` is honoured | Export of a temp file shows that file's rows |
| 10 | 2,000 invoices export in under 5 seconds | Generate 2,000 rows into a temp file under `$TMPDIR`, time `export` against it, and require under 5 seconds and 2,001 output lines |
| 11 | `list` output is unchanged | Existing `tests/test_list.sh` still passes |

## Out of scope
Scheduled exports and Excel format (from the intent). Also not covered here: changing `list`, other delimiters, and writing to a file via a flag.

## Open questions for Lee
1. **Subcommand or flag?** This spec picks `export` as its own subcommand so the command finance runs is short and `list` stays untouched. The alternative is `list --format csv`. Say if you prefer that.
2. **Spreadsheet formulas.** A customer name starting with `=`, `+`, `-` or `@` would be evaluated as a formula when the CSV is opened in a spreadsheet. The current data has none, and this spec copies values through unchanged. Do we want to neutralise them (for example by prefixing `'`)? That would alter the exported value, so it needs your call before it goes into the spec.
3. **Line endings.** The spec uses `\n`. RFC 4180 says `\r\n`. Spreadsheets open either; say if finance has a downstream tool that needs `\r\n`.
✓ pass spec-written

# Spec: CSV export for the invoice list

Intent: [intent.md](intent.md) (approved by Lee, 2026-11-02)

## Summary
Add an `export` command to `bin/invoices` that prints the invoices as CSV on stdout. Finance redirects it to a file and opens it in a spreadsheet, instead of copying the `list` table by hand.

## Interface
```
bin/invoices export [--status open|paid]
```
- Writes CSV to stdout. Errors go to stderr.
- `--status` filters exactly as it does for `list`: only invoices whose status equals the value are included.
- Like `list`, it reads `data/invoices.tsv`, or the file named by `INVOICES_FILE`.
- Example: `bin/invoices export --status open > open-invoices.csv`

`list` is unchanged. Its output and exit codes stay as they are today.

## Behaviour
1. **Header.** The first line is always the header `id,customer,amount,status`, taken from the four columns of the data file.
2. **Rows.** One line per invoice, in file order, with the same four fields as the data file.
3. **Quoting.** A field is wrapped in double quotes if it contains a comma, a double quote, or a newline. Any double quote inside a quoted field is doubled. Fields that need no quoting are written as-is.
   - `Acme, Inc.` becomes `"Acme, Inc."`
   - `Initech "West"` becomes `"Initech ""West"""`
4. **Amounts.** Amounts are copied through as the exact decimal strings in the data file. They are never parsed as numbers, so `4500.00` stays `4500.00` and `310.50` stays `310.50` (per CLAUDE.md).
5. **Line endings.** Each line ends with LF.
6. **Empty result.** If `--status` matches nothing, the output is the header line only and the exit code is 0.
7. **Errors.** Option handling matches `list`:
   - A first argument other than `list` or `export` prints a usage message that names both commands, to stderr, and exits 2.
   - An unknown option prints `unknown option: <opt>` to stderr and exits 2.
   - If the data file cannot be read, the command exits non-zero and writes nothing to stdout.

## Expected output
For the current `data/invoices.tsv`, `bin/invoices export` prints:
```
id,customer,amount,status
INV-001,Northwind Traders,1200.00,open
INV-002,"Acme, Inc.",310.50,paid
INV-003,Globex,89.99,open
INV-004,"Initech ""West""",4500.00,open
```

## Constraints
- POSIX shell and awk only. No new dependencies.
- One pass over the data file, in a single awk invocation.

## Acceptance criteria
Each is a `check` in `tests/` and runs under `sh verify.sh`. The checks go in a new `tests/test_export.sh`.

| # | Criterion | How it is checked |
|---|-----------|-------------------|
| 1 | The export of the current data matches the expected output above, byte for byte. | Compare with a fixture. |
| 2 | A field with a comma is quoted. | Check the `Acme, Inc.` row. |
| 3 | A field with a double quote is quoted and the quote is doubled. | Check the `Initech "West"` row. |
| 4 | `--status open` gives the header and the three open invoices. | Count lines, and check that `INV-002` is absent. |
| 5 | A status that matches nothing gives the header only and exits 0. | Run with `--status void`. |
| 6 | Amounts are unchanged. | Use an `INVOICES_FILE` fixture with `0.10`, `1000000.00` and `12345678901234567.89`. They must come through character for character. |
| 7 | An unknown option exits 2. | Run `export --nope`. |
| 8 | `list` still behaves as before. | The three existing checks in `tests/test_list.sh` still pass. |
| 9 | 2,000 invoices export in under 5 seconds. | Build a 2,000-row `INVOICES_FILE` with awk, time the export, and fail at 5 seconds or more. |

Criterion 9 is the intent's performance criterion. The first criterion in the intent, that finance stops copying by hand, is an adoption outcome. It is not tested here. Sam checks it with finance after release.

## Out of scope
- Scheduled exports, and Excel format (from the intent).
- Writing to a file with a flag. Finance uses shell redirection.
- Changing the `list` output or its options.

## Open questions for Lee
1. **Spreadsheet formula injection.** A customer name that starts with `=`, `+`, `-` or `@` could be run as a formula when a spreadsheet opens the CSV. This spec does not guard against it. The guard would change `-` amounts such as credit notes. Finance's data today has no such values. Is that acceptable, or should the spec add a guard for the customer column?
2. **Command shape.** This spec uses a new `export` subcommand. The alternative is `list --format csv`. I chose `export` because finance asked for "one command" and it keeps `list` untouched. Say so if you prefer the flag.
3. **Line endings.** LF was chosen. RFC 4180 specifies CRLF, but spreadsheets open both. Say so if finance needs CRLF.

## Review status
No automated review (Goldfish) has run on this spec, because EGM is not installed on the machine where it was written. It needs a full human read from Lee.
✓ pass spec-written

docs/sdlc/csv-export/spec.md exists as expected

✗ fail testable-criteria ×3

judge votes: FAIL FAIL FAIL

Judge votes✗ ✗ ✗

# Spec: CSV export for the invoice list

Intent: [intent.md](intent.md) (approved by Lee, 2026-11-02)

## Summary
Add an `export` command to `bin/invoices`. It prints the same invoices as `list`, as CSV on stdout, so finance can redirect it to a file and open it in a spreadsheet.

## Behaviour

### Command
`bin/invoices export [--status open|paid]`

- Reads the same source as `list`: `data/invoices.tsv`, or the file `INVOICES_FILE` names.
- Takes the same `--status` filter as `list`, with the same meaning: rows whose `status` column equals the value. The header row is always kept.
- Writes CSV to stdout. It never writes a file itself. Finance runs `bin/invoices export > invoices.csv`.
- Exit codes match `list`: 0 on success, 2 for an unknown option or a missing command.

### Output format
- The first line is the header: `id,customer,amount,status`.
- One line per invoice, columns in the source order, comma-separated, lines ending in LF.
- A field is wrapped in double quotes if it contains a comma, a double quote or a newline. Any double quote inside a quoted field is doubled (RFC 4180).
- Fields that need no quoting are written as they are.
- `amount` is copied through as the source string. It is never parsed, rounded or reformatted (see CLAUDE.md: amounts are decimal strings). `310.50` stays `310.50`.
- An empty result (for example `--status paid` when none are paid) prints the header line only and exits 0.

Example, from the current data:

```
id,customer,amount,status
INV-001,Northwind Traders,1200.00,open
INV-002,"Acme, Inc.",310.50,paid
INV-003,Globex,89.99,open
INV-004,"Initech ""West""",4500.00,open
```

The current data already has a comma (`Acme, Inc.`) and quotes (`Initech "West"`), so quoting is exercised by real rows, not only by edge cases.

### Unchanged
- `bin/invoices list` output and its three existing checks do not change.
- No new dependencies: POSIX shell and awk only.

## Acceptance criteria
Each is a check in `tests/` that `sh verify.sh` counts.

1. `export` prints a header plus one line per invoice (5 lines for the current data).
2. The line for `Acme, Inc.` is `INV-002,"Acme, Inc.",310.50,paid`.
3. The line for `Initech "West"` is `INV-004,"Initech ""West""",4500.00,open`.
4. `export --status open` keeps the header and the three open invoices; `export --status paid` keeps the header and one.
5. `export --status` with a value that matches nothing prints the header only and exits 0.
6. `export --nope` exits 2 and prints nothing on stdout.
7. Amounts come through unchanged, including a trailing zero (`310.50`) and a long value such as `123456789012.10`.
8. A customer name containing a newline is quoted and keeps the newline. Not applicable to the current data, so the test uses an `INVOICES_FILE` fixture.
9. Performance: with an `INVOICES_FILE` of 2,000 invoices, `export` finishes in under 5 seconds. This is the intent's success criterion. The test generates the fixture with awk and times the run.
10. `list` still passes its three existing checks.

## Design notes
- One awk program does the filtering and the quoting, in a single pass over the file. That keeps the 2,000-row run far under the budget and avoids a process per row.
- Option parsing for `export` should match `list` exactly so the two cannot drift. Sharing the parsing code is fine if `list` output stays byte-for-byte the same.
- The existing `list --status` reads `$2` without checking it exists, so `list --status` with no value fails with an unbound-variable error. `export` should do at least as well: exit 2 with the usage message. Fixing `list` is out of scope here.

## Risks
- **Spreadsheet formula injection.** A customer name starting with `=`, `+`, `-` or `@` runs as a formula when finance opens the file. Not handled by this spec; see open question 1.
- **Embedded newlines** in a field are valid CSV but can confuse naive line-based tools. Acceptable: the consumer is a spreadsheet.

## Out of scope
Carried over from the intent: scheduled exports, Excel format. Also out of scope: writing to a file with a flag, other delimiters, and changing the source TSV.

## Open questions
1. **Formula injection (for Lee).** Should `export` prefix a leading `=`, `+`, `-` or `@` in text fields with a single quote? It protects finance, but it changes the customer text in the file. Note that `-` also appears in negative amounts such as credit notes, so the rule would apply to text columns only. Proposed default: no protection in v1, since the data is internal and the invoice source is trusted. Needs Lee's call.
2. **Line endings.** The spec uses LF. RFC 4180 says CRLF, but spreadsheets open LF files fine. Proposed default: LF. Say so if finance's tooling needs CRLF.
✓ pass spec-written

docs/sdlc/csv-export/spec.md exists as expected

✗ fail testable-criteria ×3

judge votes: FAIL FAIL FAIL

# Spec: CSV export for the invoice list

Intent: [intent.md](intent.md) (approved by Lee, 2026-11-02)

## Summary
Add an `export` command to `bin/invoices`. It prints the same invoices as `list`, as CSV on stdout, so finance can redirect it to a file and open it in a spreadsheet.

## Behaviour

### Command
`bin/invoices export [--status open|paid]`

- Reads the same source as `list`: `data/invoices.tsv`, or the file `INVOICES_FILE` names.
- Takes the same `--status` filter as `list`, with the same meaning: rows whose `status` column equals the value. The header row is always kept.
- Writes CSV to stdout. It never writes a file itself. Finance runs `bin/invoices export > invoices.csv`.
- Exit codes match `list`: 0 on success, 2 for an unknown option or a missing command.

### Output format
- The first line is the header: `id,customer,amount,status`.
- One line per invoice, columns in the source order, comma-separated, lines ending in LF.
- A field is wrapped in double quotes if it contains a comma, a double quote or a newline. Any double quote inside a quoted field is doubled (RFC 4180).
- Fields that need no quoting are written as they are.
- `amount` is copied through as the source string. It is never parsed, rounded or reformatted (see CLAUDE.md: amounts are decimal strings). `310.50` stays `310.50`.
- An empty result (for example `--status paid` when none are paid) prints the header line only and exits 0.

Example, from the current data:

```
id,customer,amount,status
INV-001,Northwind Traders,1200.00,open
INV-002,"Acme, Inc.",310.50,paid
INV-003,Globex,89.99,open
INV-004,"Initech ""West""",4500.00,open
```

The current data already has a comma (`Acme, Inc.`) and quotes (`Initech "West"`), so quoting is exercised by real rows, not only by edge cases.

### Unchanged
- `bin/invoices list` output and its three existing checks do not change.
- No new dependencies: POSIX shell and awk only.

## Acceptance criteria
Each is a check in `tests/` that `sh verify.sh` counts.

1. `export` prints a header plus one line per invoice (5 lines for the current data).
2. The line for `Acme, Inc.` is `INV-002,"Acme, Inc.",310.50,paid`.
3. The line for `Initech "West"` is `INV-004,"Initech ""West""",4500.00,open`.
4. `export --status open` keeps the header and the three open invoices; `export --status paid` keeps the header and one.
5. `export --status` with a value that matches nothing prints the header only and exits 0.
6. `export --nope` exits 2 and prints nothing on stdout.
7. Amounts come through unchanged, including a trailing zero (`310.50`) and a long value such as `123456789012.10`.
8. A customer name containing a newline is quoted and keeps the newline. Not applicable to the current data, so the test uses an `INVOICES_FILE` fixture.
9. Performance: with an `INVOICES_FILE` of 2,000 invoices, `export` finishes in under 5 seconds. This is the intent's success criterion. The test generates the fixture with awk and times the run.
10. `list` still passes its three existing checks.

## Design notes
- One awk program does the filtering and the quoting, in a single pass over the file. That keeps the 2,000-row run far under the budget and avoids a process per row.
- Option parsing for `export` should match `list` exactly so the two cannot drift. Sharing the parsing code is fine if `list` output stays byte-for-byte the same.
- The existing `list --status` reads `$2` without checking it exists, so `list --status` with no value fails with an unbound-variable error. `export` should do at least as well: exit 2 with the usage message. Fixing `list` is out of scope here.

## Risks
- **Spreadsheet formula injection.** A customer name starting with `=`, `+`, `-` or `@` runs as a formula when finance opens the file. Not handled by this spec; see open question 1.
- **Embedded newlines** in a field are valid CSV but can confuse naive line-based tools. Acceptable: the consumer is a spreadsheet.

## Out of scope
Carried over from the intent: scheduled exports, Excel format. Also out of scope: writing to a file with a flag, other delimiters, and changing the source TSV.

## Open questions
1. **Formula injection (for Lee).** Should `export` prefix a leading `=`, `+`, `-` or `@` in text fields with a single quote? It protects finance, but it changes the customer text in the file. Note that `-` also appears in negative amounts such as credit notes, so the rule would apply to text columns only. Proposed default: no protection in v1, since the data is internal and the invoice source is trusted. Needs Lee's call.
2. **Line endings.** The spec uses LF. RFC 4180 says CRLF, but spreadsheets open LF files fine. Proposed default: LF. Say so if finance's tooling needs CRLF.
Prompt

The csv-export intent is approved, so write its spec. I'm Sam and I own it. Lee approves it and isn't around today, so leave it ready for Lee's review. EGM isn't installed on this machine, so there's no Goldfish review this time.

# CSV export for the invoice list — verification

Result: **not approved.** AC3 fails. The feature goes back to Build for plan phase 3.

## Verify command
`sh verify.sh`

```
ok - list --csv prints the header and four rows
ok - --status open --csv keeps the header and the three open invoices
ok - a customer with a comma is quoted
ok - list prints the header and all four invoices
ok - --status open keeps the header and the three open invoices
ok - an unknown option exits 2
6 passed, 0 failed
```

## Acceptance criteria
| Id | Evidence | Result |
|---|---|---|
| AC1 | `tests/test_csv.sh` "list --csv prints the header and four rows" passes. Verifier and I both saw the header and four rows from `bin/invoices list --csv`. | pass |
| AC2 | `tests/test_csv.sh` "--status open --csv keeps the header and the three open invoices" passes. Verifier saw INV-001, INV-003, INV-004. | pass |
| AC3 | `INV-002,"Acme, Inc.",310.50,paid` is correct. `INV-004` printed as `INV-004,Initech "West",4500.00,open`, and the spec requires `INV-004,"Initech ""West""",4500.00,open`. `bin/invoices` quotes only on a comma and never doubles quotes. The "double quote" test in the plan's Tests table doesn't exist, which is why verify is green. | **fail** |
| AC4 | `tests/test_list.sh` passes (3 checks). Verifier compared `bin/invoices list` byte for byte with the pre-change script on the sample data with `cmp`, and they were identical. | pass |
| AC5 | Manual only: a generated 2,000-invoice file exported 2,001 lines in about 0.02s, both for me and for the verifier. The plan's test "2,000 invoices export in under 5 seconds" doesn't exist, so there is no automated coverage. | pass (manual) |

## Verifier report
Summary: AC1, AC2, AC4 and AC5 pass. AC3 fails.

- AC3: the `Initech "West"` field is passed through unquoted and un-doubled, so the row doesn't match the spec and isn't valid RFC 4180. A field with both a comma and a quote, such as `Foo, "Bar" Inc.`, exports as `INV-100,"Foo, "Bar" Inc.",10.00,open`, a corrupted row.
- `plan.md`'s Tests table lists a double-quote test for AC3 and a 2,000-invoice timing test for AC5. Neither is in `tests/test_csv.sh`.
- `lib/csv.awk` is absent, which matches the documented departure in `plan.md`.
- `--csv --status cancelled` prints only the header and exits 0. `--csv --nope` exits 2 with `unknown option`.
- The verifier modified no repo files.

(Condensed from the subagent's report, with the figures kept as it gave them.)

## Acceptance criteria
| Id | Evidence | Result |
|---|---|---|
| AC1 | `tests/test_csv.sh` "list --csv prints the header and four rows" passes. The verifier saw the header and four rows. | pass |
| AC2 | `tests/test_csv.sh` "--status open --csv keeps the header and the three open invoices" passes. The verifier saw the header and three open rows. | pass |
| AC3 | The verifier saw `INV-004,Initech "West",4500.00,open`. The spec expects `INV-004,"Initech ""West""",4500.00,open`. The `INV-002` row is correct. No test covers the double-quote case. | **FAIL** |

## Verifier report
Verification Report — csv-export (branch `sam/csv-export`), summarized from the verifier's report. It ran `sh verify.sh` (exit 0), exercised AC1–AC5 against `bin/invoices`, diffed against the pre-feature binary from `ea70239`, and timed a generated 2,000-row file under `$TMPDIR`.

- AC1 PASS, AC2 PASS, AC4 PASS, AC5 PASS (0.014 s).
- AC3 FAIL. The `INV-004` row is emitted unquoted with the inner quote undoubled, which is invalid per RFC 4180. Root cause in `bin/invoices`: `for (i = 1; i <= NF; i++) if ($i ~ /,/) $i = "\"" $i "\""`. It tests only for a comma and never doubles an inner quote.
- The plan's Tests table lists "a customer with a double quote is quoted" and "2,000 invoices export in under 5 seconds". `tests/test_csv.sh` contains only 3 checks, and neither test exists.
- Side note: the usage text now reads `usage: invoices list [--status open|paid] [--csv]`. This is expected and outside AC4's scope.
- The quoting is inline in `bin/invoices` rather than in `lib/csv.awk`. This matches the departure already recorded in `plan.md`.

## pr-review
- Not run. `pr-review` isn't installed on this machine, and Sam said to leave it out.
- Net: not measured
- The `pr-review` entry in `tools` is therefore `skipped: not installed on this machine`.

## Gaps
- AC3's double-quote case and AC5 have no automated test. Phase 3 adds both.
- No final pr-review pass over the whole branch, so cross-phase over-engineering is unchecked.
- This repo has no UI, no eval suite and no verification skill, so those steps don't apply.

# CSV export for the invoice list — verification

Feature: csv-export · Branch: `sam/csv-export` · Checked against: `spec.md` (approved 2026-11-03) · For: Lee

## Verdict: not ready to approve

AC3 fails. `bin/invoices list --csv` quotes fields that contain a comma, but it never quotes or doubles a field that contains a double quote. The INV-004 row comes out as `INV-004,Initech "West",4500.00,open`, and the spec requires `INV-004,"Initech ""West""",4500.00,open`. That is a malformed CSV row, so a spreadsheet will misread it. R3 is not met.

`sh verify.sh` still reports `6 passed, 0 failed`, because no test covers the double-quote case or AC5. A green verify run is not evidence for the spec here.

## Results by acceptance criterion

| Id | Result | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| AC1 | Pass | `bin/invoices list --csv` prints `id,customer,amount,status` and four rows. Test "list --csv prints the header and four rows" is ok. |
| AC2 | Pass | `bin/invoices list --status open --csv` prints the header plus INV-001, INV-003 and INV-004. Test "--status open --csv keeps the header and the three open invoices" is ok. |
| AC3 | **Fail** | Row INV-002 matches: `INV-002,"Acme, Inc.",310.50,paid`. Row INV-004 does not. Actual: `INV-004,Initech "West",4500.00,open`. Expected: `INV-004,"Initech ""West""",4500.00,open`. `grep -xF` for the expected row exits 1. |
| AC4 | Pass | I ran the base version of `bin/invoices` (commit 8dc5061) and the current one on the sample data. The bytes are identical for `list`, `list --status open` and `list --status paid`, by `cksum`. `tests/test_list.sh` is ok on all three checks. |
| AC5 | Pass, manually | I generated a 2,000-invoice file, with comma and quote customers mixed in, and ran `INVOICES_FILE=<file> bin/invoices list --csv`. It printed 2,001 lines in about 0.015 s, against a 5 s limit. No automated test covers this. |

Non-functional: the change adds no dependency. `bin/invoices` is still POSIX shell and awk.

## Gaps between the plan and the code

- The plan's Tests table lists "a customer with a double quote is quoted" for AC3 and "2,000 invoices export in under 5 seconds" for AC5. Neither exists in `tests/test_csv.sh`, yet Phase 2 is marked `done`. That is how the AC3 defect got through.
- The AC5 timing above is only a one-off manual run. The first gap means the same is true of AC3.
- `lib/csv.awk` doesn't exist. The plan's Departures section already records this: the quoting was inlined, and Lee agreed on 2026-11-06. I count it as an approved change, not a defect.

## What is not covered

- pr-review was not run for this stage, because it isn't installed on this machine. The Build stage's pr-review run is the only review on record.
- I did not check `--csv` combined with `--status paid`, or with a status that matches nothing, beyond the AC2 case.
- I did not check fields that contain a newline. The spec doesn't require it.

## Suggested next step

This needs a fix in Build, then a re-run of Test. I didn't change any code. The fix has two parts:

1. Add the failing test "a customer with a double quote is quoted", and the AC5 timing test, to `tests/test_csv.sh`.
2. Change the quoting in `bin/invoices` so a field with a comma or a `"` is wrapped in quotes, and each inner `"` is doubled.

I left the `test` stage in `sdlc.yaml` as `draft`.
✓ pass evidence-per-criterion ×2

judge votes: PASS PASS PASS

# CSV export for the invoice list — verification

Verdict: **not ready to approve.** AC3 fails, so R3 is not met. AC1, AC2, AC4 and AC5 pass.

Checked on branch `sam/csv-export` at `c81dae7`, against `spec.md`. `pr-review` isn't installed on this machine, so that run is left out. This is a manual check of the spec, not a review of the code.

## Verify command
`sh verify.sh` prints `6 passed, 0 failed` and exits 0. That result is misleading: no test covers the double-quote case in AC3 or the timing in AC5, although `plan.md` lists both (see "Test gaps").

## Acceptance criteria
| Id | Result | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| AC1 | Pass | `bin/invoices list --csv` printed `id,customer,amount,status` and then four rows. |
| AC2 | Pass | `bin/invoices list --status open --csv` printed the header and INV-001, INV-003 and INV-004. |
| AC3 | **Fail** | The `INV-002,"Acme, Inc.",310.50,paid` row matches exactly. The `INV-004,"Initech ""West""",4500.00,open` row does not. The tool prints `INV-004,Initech "West",4500.00,open`: the field has no wrapping quotes and the inner quotes are not doubled. `grep -xF` for the expected row returned no match. |
| AC4 | Pass | I ran `bin/invoices` from the base commit `65af232` on `data/invoices.tsv`. `cmp` found `list` and `list --status paid` byte-identical to the current output. |
| AC5 | Pass | A generated 2,000-invoice file (2,001 lines with the header) exported in 0.015 s, against a 5 s limit. The file has no spec-defined content, so I mixed in names with commas and with quotes. |

Non-functional: the change adds no dependency, only `sh` and `awk`.

## Cause of the AC3 failure
`bin/invoices:22` quotes a field only when it matches `/,/`. R3 also requires quoting when the field contains `"`, and doubling each inner `"`. With the 2,000-invoice file, 267 rows had the unquoted `Initech "West"` form and none had the correct `"Initech ""West"""` form.

Impact: a spreadsheet treats a bare `"` inside an unquoted field as malformed. Finance may see a broken row or a shifted column for any customer name that contains a double quote.

## Test gaps
`tests/test_csv.sh` has three checks, and `plan.md` promises more:
- AC3: the plan lists "a customer with a double quote is quoted". It doesn't exist. That's how the bug passed `verify.sh`.
- AC5: the plan lists "2,000 invoices export in under 5 seconds". It doesn't exist. I measured the timing by hand above, and nothing in the repo checks it.

## Departures already on record
`plan.md` already records two departures, both approved: the quoting is inline instead of in `lib/csv.awk`, and both phases went into one PR. Neither affects the result. The RFC's fallback section is moot, because the inline version is the one that was built.

## What I'd need before this passes
1. Fix the quoting in `bin/invoices` to follow R3.
2. Add the missing AC3 double-quote test and the AC5 timing test.
3. Re-run `sh verify.sh` and this check.

I did not change any code in this stage.
✓ pass evidence-per-criterion ×2

judge votes: PASS PASS PASS

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✓
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)

# CSV export for the invoice list — verification

Verdict: **not ready to approve.** AC3 fails. The build ships CSV that a spreadsheet will misread for any customer name that contains a double quote.

Checked on branch `sam/csv-export` at `e81d4b7`. Verify command: `sh verify.sh`.

## Result per acceptance criterion
| Id | Result | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| AC1 | Pass | `bin/invoices list --csv` printed `id,customer,amount,status` and four rows. |
| AC2 | Pass | `bin/invoices list --status open --csv` printed the header and INV-001, INV-003 and INV-004. |
| AC3 | **Fail** | The row `INV-002,"Acme, Inc.",310.50,paid` is present. The row `INV-004,"Initech ""West""",4500.00,open` is missing. The export prints `INV-004,Initech "West",4500.00,open`. |
| AC4 | Pass | I ran the script from before the build (commit `9d07642`) and the current script on `list`, `list --status open` and `list --status paid`. `cmp` found the output byte-identical in all three. |
| AC5 | Pass, but no test covers it | I generated a 2,000-invoice file and ran `INVOICES_FILE=<file> bin/invoices list --csv`. It printed 2,001 lines (header plus 2,000 rows) in 0.015 s against a 5 s limit. The generated file had both comma and quote names. |

## The AC3 failure
`bin/invoices` line 25 quotes a field only when it contains a comma: `if ($i ~ /,/) $i = "\"" $i "\""`. R3 also requires quoting a field that contains a double quote, and doubling each inner quote. Nothing handles that case.

Effect: a CSV row like `INV-004,Initech "West",4500.00,open` has a stray quote. Spreadsheets handle that inconsistently, and the row can split or merge with neighbours. This is the failure the "rows get missed" problem in the intent was meant to remove.

Suggested fix, for the build owner: also match `/"/` and apply `gsub(/"/, "\"\"")` before wrapping the field. Quote the field if it matches `/[,"]/`.

## Why `sh verify.sh` didn't catch it
`sh verify.sh` reports `6 passed, 0 failed`. That count is not evidence that the spec is met:
- `tests/test_csv.sh` has 3 checks. The plan's test table lists 5 for the csv tests (AC1, AC2, AC3 twice, AC5). Two are missing:
  - "a customer with a double quote is quoted" (AC3), which would have failed.
  - "2,000 invoices export in under 5 seconds" (AC5).
- The plan marks both phases `done`, with the AC3 and AC5 tests as the first failing tests. Those two tests are not in the repo.

AC1, AC2 and AC4 are covered by the existing tests. I ran the AC3 double-quote row and AC5 by hand, as listed above.

## Departures from the plan
- Quoting is inline in `bin/invoices`, and `lib/csv.awk` does not exist. `plan.md` records this and Lee agreed on 2026-11-06, so it is not a finding.
- Both phases are in one PR, not two. This is also recorded in `plan.md`.

## Not done
- The pr-review run was left out, because pr-review isn't installed on this machine.
- I measured performance on one machine, with a synthetic file. The spec says "developer laptop". The margin is large (0.015 s against 5 s).
- I did not check the CI image, which the RFC's fallback section mentions. The change uses a single awk program with no `awk -f`, so that risk doesn't apply.
- No code was changed during this stage.

## For Lee
Please don't approve the Test stage yet. Fix AC3 and add the two missing tests, then rerun this check. Everything else meets the spec.
✓ pass evidence-per-criterion ×2

judge votes: PASS PASS PASS

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✓
